# Phase 5.7 V1 audit — cross-repo (engine `ql-bindings-node` + IDE `extensions/quantlab/src/quantbook/`)

## Context

This is the FIRST IDE binding to the quantbook engine since the engine has existed. V1 deliberately scoped to a minimum vertical slice: napi-rs cdylib exposing `CollabSession` for a TS round-trip. NO Transport binding, NO multi-window, NO real UI.

**Audit targets (both repos)**:

- Engine (quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine, `feat/quantbook-engine`): HEAD `677ee03ee8b` — V1 ship of `ql-bindings-node` crate.
- IDE (quantlab/quantlab, `feat/visualise-v1`): HEAD `1a7fc8bbe3f` — V1 ship of `extensions/quantlab/src/quantbook/` + demo command + 10-test mocha suite.

**Tests at ship**:
- Engine workspace: in-progress as of audit start; expected 4460+N/0 (no regressions from prior V2 V4 V1 baseline of 4460).
- Engine binding crate: 2 Rust unit tests pass.
- IDE-side: 10/10 Mocha tests pass when engine binary present.

**Predecessor**: V2 V4 V1 step 5 closure (`397676899fb`) + docs finalize (`51e4117002e`).

## V1 surface shipped

### Engine `ql-bindings-node` (`crates/ql-bindings-node/`)

- `Cargo.toml`: cdylib + rlib, napi-rs 3.x workspace deps
- `build.rs`: `napi_build::setup()` only
- `src/lib.rs`:
  - `version() -> String` — smoke
  - `peer_id_from_bigint(&BigInt) -> Result<PeerId>` — 4-failure-mode validator (sign, lossless, peer == 0, engine errors)
  - `struct CollabSession` wrapping `ql_collab::CollabSession`, exposing: constructor, `fromSnapshot`, `appendPutValue` (V1 single-Op convenience), `exportBytes`, `mergeBytes`, `opCount`, `pendingOpCount`, `hasPendingFlush`, `peerId`
- 2 Rust unit tests in `#[cfg(test)] mod tests`

### IDE `extensions/quantlab/src/quantbook/` + commands

- `loader.ts` — `resolveEnginePath` (env var or walk-up to find IDE root via `package.json`'s `name: "code-oss-dev"`), `loadQuantbookEngine` (uses `process.dlopen` directly), `_resetQuantbookEngineCacheForTests`, `quantbookHostInfo`
- `types.ts` — TS declaration mirror of the napi surface
- `session.ts` — thin wrappers `createSession`, `sessionFromSnapshot`, `quantbookEngineVersion`
- `commands/quantbookCommands.ts` — registers `quantlab.quantbookDemo`; modal+output-channel UX
- `test/quantbook-roundtrip.test.ts` — 10 mocha tests with skip-on-missing-prereq pattern + 60s suiteSetup preload

## What to audit (BOTH lanes deeply)

### High-priority focus

1. **Cross-FFI panic safety** (Audit Rule 4 trigger). The V1 plan flagged this as HIGH risk. SMOKE TEST CAUGHT one panic: `assert_ne!(0, ...)` in `PeerId(0)` rejection panicked AND aborted Node ("failed to initiate panic, error 5, aborting"). Fix shipped: pre-validate `peer_id == 0` in `peer_id_from_bigint`. Are there OTHER engine paths reachable from the binding that use `assert_*!`, `panic!`, `unwrap`, or `expect`? Sweep `ql-collab` and `ql-oplog` for assertion-based input validation. If any panic-style validation is reachable from the napi entry points, surface it as HIGH/MEDIUM.

2. **`BigInt` round-trip safety**. `peer_id_from_bigint` handles sign bit, lossless, zero. Does it miss any failure mode? What if napi-rs converts a non-BigInt JS value (e.g., `123` Number) into a BigInt internally? Does the validator still reject? Test boundary cases:
   - `1n << 64n` (one past u64::MAX) — should be lossless=false
   - `1n << 65n` — `get_u64()` behavior?
   - `9223372036854775807n` (i64::MAX, fits in u64)
   - `9223372036854775808n` (i64::MAX + 1, still u64)
   - Negative BigInt at u64::MAX boundary
   - JS `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` if user passes Number instead of BigInt (TS type errors but runtime may pass it through)

3. **`Uint8Array` ownership at FFI boundary**. `exportBytes` returns `Uint8Array::from(bytes)` which clones. `mergeBytes(bytes: Uint8Array)` does `bytes.as_ref()` which references the JS-owned buffer. What happens if JS code holds the Uint8Array, calls `mergeBytes(arr)`, and during the async (or rather sync) call modifies `arr` from another thread? V1 is sync so concurrent mutation is unlikely from JS, BUT what about a `SharedArrayBuffer`-backed `Uint8Array` from a Worker?

4. **Send + Sync on the napi wrapper**. `ql_collab::CollabSession` is `Send + !Sync`. The napi wrapper struct `CollabSession { inner: CoreCollabSession }` inherits these. napi-rs class instances live on the JS heap; method calls happen on the JS thread (single-threaded by default). Is there a multi-thread napi pattern (`ThreadsafeFunction`, async runtime invocation) that could call `&mut self` methods concurrently? Per Rule 4: verify the actual constraint, don't claim from intuition. Find napi-rs's documented threading model for class instance methods.

5. **Loader path resolution robustness**. `findIdeRepoRoot` walks UP looking for `package.json` with `name: "code-oss-dev"`. What if:
   - Someone forks code-oss-dev under a different name
   - User has multiple checkouts (e.g., a worktree)
   - The IDE repo is renamed
   - Workspace structure changes
   The fallback path (when `findIdeRepoRoot` returns undefined) uses 6 `..` hops from `__dirname`. Is that correct for all `__dirname` shapes (src vs out vs bundled)?

6. **Loader load-bearing claims (CLAUDE.md no-fallback)**. The docstring says "fallback here is path-resolution, not error-masking." Is that accurate? Every failure mode (env var path missing, default path missing, file not loadable, partial exports) should surface a LOUD diagnostic. Trace each path:
   - Empty/missing env var → fall through to default
   - Env var set but file missing → SHOULD LOUD-FAIL (verify)
   - Default discovery fails (no code-oss-dev ancestor) → uses 6-hop coarse fallback (THIS IS A FALLBACK — flag if it could silently succeed pointing at the wrong place)
   - `process.dlopen` throws → LOUD with platform + Node ABI
   - `dlopen` succeeds but exports missing → LOUD with key list

7. **`as unknown as NodeJS.Module` cast**. Documented Node pattern but the local lint rule was bypassed. Is there a SAFE alternative that doesn't compromise the cast? E.g., symlink to `.node` extension + use `require()`. If the cast is genuinely unavoidable, the audit should mark it ACK; otherwise propose the alternative.

8. **Skip-on-missing-prereq test pattern**. The 10 mocha tests skip if the engine binary is missing. Is the skip logged loudly enough that a CI run won't silently pass with no engine? Mirror the qviz-daemon-client.test.ts pattern more closely if needed.

9. **`package.json` test glob**. Updated to include `quantbook*.test.js` alongside `qviz*.test.js`. Any other test name patterns this misses (e.g., future `quantbook-transport-*.test.js`)?

10. **Documentation drift**. The module-docs claim "napi-rs handles panic safety via catch_unwind" — empirically that's NOT true for the engine's `assert_ne!` path. The docstring needs updating to reflect the V1 smoke finding.

### Cross-cutting audit-discipline reminders

Per `~/.claude/projects/.../memory/quantbook_engine_audit_discipline.md`:
- Rule 4: negative trait claims need positive compile proof OR per-field walk. Apply to ANY `Send`/`Sync`/`!Sync` claim in either repo.
- Rule 3: range-aware fn ships need lex+parse+bind+eval coverage. N/A for this step (no formula work).
- Rule 1: stop fresh-session reminders. N/A.
- Rule 2: parallel Codex+Opus per phase/wave/step. THIS audit is the Rule 2 instance.

### CLAUDE.md cross-cutting

- **No-fallback rule**: scan both repos for any `try/catch` that swallows errors, `value || default` that masks missing config, "graceful degradation" silent paths, `default` parameters hiding required args, silent retries, `rescue nil`, `|| true`.
- **Verify before claiming**: any code comment claiming "verified" or "tested" should be backed by a compile-time check OR a test. Flag bare claims.

## Verdict format

Standard 5-bucket: HIGH / MEDIUM / LOW / NEEDS_VERIFICATION / OBSERVATIONS. Mark each finding with:
- Severity
- Repo (engine OR IDE)
- File:line citation
- What's wrong (be specific)
- Closure suggestion (precise; do NOT just say "fix it")
- Whether closure should happen in cycle OR defer to V2

Convergent findings (Codex + Opus agree) get bumped one severity tier per the discipline established in earlier audits.

## Audit closure cycle

After both Codex + Opus reports land, synthesize:
- Convergent findings (priority 1)
- HIGH severity unique to one lane (priority 2)
- MEDIUM unique to one lane (priority 3 — case-by-case)
- LOW + OBSERVATIONS (priority 4 — selective)
- V2 backlog candidates (deferred)

Close in-cycle:
- Engine binding code fixes
- IDE binding code fixes
- Doc updates (loader.ts module doc, ql-bindings-node lib.rs module doc, .plans/_active.md)

Cross-repo closure commits happen in BOTH repos as needed.

# VS Code Copilot Instructions

## Project Overview

Visual Studio Code is built with a layered architecture using TypeScript, web APIs and Electron, combining web technologies with native app capabilities. The codebase is organized into key architectural layers:

### Root Folders
- `src/`: Main TypeScript source code with unit tests in `src/vs/*/test/` folders
- `build/`: Build scripts and CI/CD tools
- `extensions/`: Built-in extensions that ship with VS Code
- `test/`: Integration tests and test infrastructure
- `scripts/`: Development and build scripts
- `resources/`: Static resources (icons, themes, etc.)
- `out/`: Compiled JavaScript output (generated during build)

### Core Architecture (`src/` folder)
- `src/vs/base/` - Foundation utilities and cross-platform abstractions
- `src/vs/platform/` - Platform services and dependency injection infrastructure
- `src/vs/editor/` - Text editor implementation with language services, syntax highlighting, and editing features
- `src/vs/workbench/` - Main application workbench for web and desktop
  - `workbench/browser/` - Core workbench UI components (parts, layout, actions)
  - `workbench/services/` - Service implementations
  - `workbench/contrib/` - Feature contributions (git, debug, search, terminal, etc.)
  - `workbench/api/` - Extension host and VS Code API implementation
- `src/vs/code/` - Electron main process specific implementation
- `src/vs/server/` - Server specific implementation

The core architecture follows these principles:
- **Layered architecture** - from `base`, `platform`, `editor`, to `workbench`
- **Dependency injection** - Services are injected through constructor parameters
- **Contribution model** - Features contribute to registries and extension points
- **Cross-platform compatibility** - Abstractions separate platform-specific code

### Built-in Extensions (`extensions/` folder)
The `extensions/` directory contains first-party extensions that ship with VS Code:
- **Language support** - `typescript-language-features/`, `html-language-features/`, `css-language-features/`, etc.
- **Core features** - `git/`, `debug-auto-launch/`, `emmet/`, `markdown-language-features/`
- **Themes** - `theme-*` folders for default color themes
- **Development tools** - `extension-editing/`, `vscode-api-tests/`

Each extension follows the standard VS Code extension structure with `package.json`, TypeScript sources, and contribution points to extend the workbench through the Extension API.

### Finding Related Code
1. **Semantic search first**: Use file search for general concepts
2. **Grep for exact strings**: Use grep for error messages or specific function names
3. **Follow imports**: Check what files import the problematic module
4. **Check test files**: Often reveal usage patterns and expected behavior

## Validating TypeScript changes

MANDATORY: Always check the `VS Code - Build` watch task output via #runTasks/getTaskOutput for compilation errors before running ANY script or declaring work complete, then fix all compilation errors before moving forward.

- NEVER run tests if there are compilation errors
- NEVER use `npm run compile` to compile TypeScript files but call #runTasks/getTaskOutput instead

### TypeScript compilation steps
- Monitor the `VS Code - Build` task outputs for real-time compilation errors as you make changes
- This task runs `Core - Build` and `Ext - Build` to incrementally compile VS Code TypeScript sources and built-in extensions
- Start the task if it's not already running in the background

### TypeScript validation steps
- Use the run test tool if you need to run tests. If that tool is not available, then you can use `scripts/test.sh` (or `scripts\test.bat` on Windows) for unit tests (add `--grep <pattern>` to filter tests) or `scripts/test-integration.sh` (or `scripts\test-integration.bat` on Windows) for integration tests (integration tests end with .integrationTest.ts or are in /extensions/).
- Use `npm run valid-layers-check` to check for layering issues

## Coding Guidelines

### Indentation

We use tabs, not spaces.

### Naming Conventions

- Use PascalCase for `type` names
- Use PascalCase for `enum` values
- Use camelCase for `function` and `method` names
- Use camelCase for `property` names and `local variables`
- Use whole words in names when possible

### Types

- Do not export `types` or `functions` unless you need to share it across multiple components
- Do not introduce new `types` or `values` to the global namespace

### Comments

- Use JSDoc style comments for `functions`, `interfaces`, `enums`, and `classes`

### Strings

- Use "double quotes" for strings shown to the user that need to be externalized (localized)
- Use 'single quotes' otherwise
- All strings visible to the user need to be externalized using the `vs/nls` module
- Externalized strings must not use string concatenation. Use placeholders instead (`{0}`).

### UI labels
- Use title-style capitalization for command labels, buttons and menu items (each word is capitalized).
- Don't capitalize prepositions of four or fewer letters unless it's the first or last word (e.g. "in", "with", "for").

### Style

- Use arrow functions `=>` over anonymous function expressions
- Only surround arrow function parameters when necessary. For example, `(x) => x + x` is wrong but the following are correct:

```typescript
x => x + x
(x, y) => x + y
<T>(x: T, y: T) => x === y
```

- Always surround loop and conditional bodies with curly braces
- Open curly braces always go on the same line as whatever necessitates them
- Parenthesized constructs should have no surrounding whitespace. A single space follows commas, colons, and semicolons in those constructs. For example:

```typescript
for (let i = 0, n = str.length; i < 10; i++) {
    if (x < 10) {
        foo();
    }
}
function f(x: number, y: string): void { }
```

- Whenever possible, use in top-level scopes `export function x(…) {…}` instead of `export const x = (…) => {…}`. One advantage of using the `function` keyword is that the stack-trace shows a good name when debugging.

### Code Quality

- All files must include Microsoft copyright header
- Prefer `async` and `await` over `Promise` and `then` calls
- All user facing messages must be localized using the applicable localization framework (for example `nls.localize()` method)
- Don't add tests to the wrong test suite (e.g., adding to end of file instead of inside relevant suite)
- Look for existing test patterns before creating new structures
- Use `describe` and `test` consistently with existing patterns
- Prefer regex capture groups with names over numbered capture groups.
- If you create any temporary new files, scripts, or helper files for iteration, clean up these files by removing them at the end of the task
- Never duplicate imports. Always reuse existing imports if they are present.
- Do not use `any` or `unknown` as the type for variables, parameters, or return values unless absolutely necessary. If they need type annotations, they should have proper types or interfaces defined.
- When adding file watching, prefer correlated file watchers (via fileService.createWatcher) to shared ones.
- When adding tooltips to UI elements, prefer the use of IHoverService service.

     1	//! `ql-bindings-node` — VS Code extension binding (Phase 5.7 V1).
     2	//!
     3	//! **Phase 5.7 V1 (2026-05-22, this ship):** binds `CollabSession` for the
     4	//! quantlab VS Code fork's extension host. Replaces the Phase-6+ reserved
     5	//! placeholder this crate used to be.
     6	//!
     7	//! ## Architecture
     8	//!
     9	//! cdylib + napi-rs 3.x. The `#[napi]` derive macros generate the
    10	//! JS-facing class shape from the Rust struct. napi-rs handles:
    11	//!   - JS class lifecycle (`new`, finalize)
    12	//!   - `BigInt <-> u64` conversion for `PeerId`
    13	//!   - `Uint8Array <-> Vec<u8>` for snapshot bytes (zero-copy on read,
    14	//!     clone on write per napi-rs default)
    15	//!   - panic safety: napi-rs wraps each `#[napi]` entry point in a
    16	//!     `catch_unwind` and surfaces panics as JS exceptions (no Node
    17	//!     process crash).
    18	//!
    19	//! ## V1 surface
    20	//!
    21	//! Single class `CollabSession` exposing the minimum methods that let
    22	//! TS code drive a round-trip:
    23	//!   - `new(peerId: bigint)`: wraps `ql_collab::CollabSession::new`
    24	//!   - static `fromSnapshot(peerId: bigint, bytes: Uint8Array)`:
    25	//!     wraps `from_snapshot`
    26	//!   - `appendPutValue(sheet, row, col, value)`: convenience for V1
    27	//!     (single Op variant; full Op enum binding deferred to V2)
    28	//!   - `exportBytes() -> Uint8Array`: snapshot
    29	//!   - `mergeBytes(bytes: Uint8Array) -> number`: returns merged op count
    30	//!   - `opCount() -> number`
    31	//!   - `pendingOpCount() -> number` (V2 V4 V1 step 2 helper)
    32	//!   - `hasPendingFlush() -> boolean` (V2 V3 step 3 helper)
    33	//!   - `peerId() -> bigint`
    34	//!
    35	//! ## V1 deferred (see `.plans/_active.md`)
    36	//!
    37	//! Transport binding (LoopbackTransport, WebSocketTransport),
    38	//! undo/redo, presence, full Op enum, format IDs, `rebuild_workbook`
    39	//! (D-3 production-visible closure), all flush_* methods. V2 picks
    40	//! these up.
    41	//!
    42	//! ## Send + Sync
    43	//!
    44	//! `CollabSession` is `Send + !Sync` (verified per V2 V4 V1 step 3 +
    45	//! audit-discipline Rule 4 in the engine's audit memory). napi-rs's
    46	//! generated class wraps each instance in a per-JS-object slot
    47	//! accessed under napi's internal locking; we don't need `Sync` for
    48	//! the binding to work — JS code can hold the object across an `await`
    49	//! but cannot call methods concurrently (napi enforces this).
    50	
    51	#![deny(clippy::all)]
    52	#![allow(clippy::missing_safety_doc)] // napi-rs generated wrappers
    53	
    54	use napi::bindgen_prelude::*;
    55	use napi_derive::napi;
    56	
    57	use ql_collab::CollabSession as CoreCollabSession;
    58	use ql_oplog::CellWireValue;
    59	use ql_oplog::Op;
    60	use ql_types::PeerId;
    61	
    62	/// **Phase 5.7 V1 (2026-05-22):** smoke method exposing the binding's
    63	/// own version. The IDE's loader uses this to verify the loaded
    64	/// `.node` file matches the engine version it expects. V2 will add a
    65	/// proper version-mismatch error path.
    66	#[napi]
    67	pub fn version() -> String {
    68	    env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION").to_string()
    69	}
    70	
    71	/// Helper: convert a JS `BigInt` to `PeerId` with explicit rejection of
    72	/// FOUR failure modes:
    73	///   - negative BigInt (signed bit set) — PeerId is u64-domain
    74	///   - BigInt doesn't fit in u64 (lossless = false)
    75	///   - `peer_id == 0` — Loro's LEGACY_PEER sentinel; rejected with a
    76	///     PROACTIVE check here (the engine's `CollabSession::new` would
    77	///     hit `assert_ne!` and panic, which napi-rs 3.x doesn't reliably
    78	///     catch into a JS exception — observed during V1 smoke test:
    79	///     "failed to initiate panic, error 5, aborting"). Pre-validate
    80	///     in the FFI boundary to keep Node alive.
    81	///
    82	/// napi-rs 3.x `BigInt::get_u64()` returns `(sign_bit: bool, value: u64,
    83	/// lossless: bool)`. `sign_bit == true` indicates a negative BigInt.
    84	/// Per CLAUDE.md no-fallback rule: each failure mode surfaces a distinct
    85	/// error message so IDE consumers can show the right user-facing copy.
    86	///
    87	/// **Phase 5.7 V1 smoke-test finding (2026-05-22)**: the original
    88	/// version of this helper allowed `peer_id == 0` through, expecting
    89	/// `CollabSession::new`'s `assert_ne!(0, 0)` to surface a clean error.
    90	/// In practice the panic ABORTED the Node process. Lesson:
    91	/// engine-level `assert_*!` is NOT a safe boundary for FFI. Every
    92	/// engine method that uses `assert_*!` for input validation needs a
    93	/// proactive pre-check at the FFI layer. V2 will sweep the engine for
    94	/// similar assertions (PeerId(0) is the only known one today).
    95	fn peer_id_from_bigint(peer_id: &BigInt) -> Result<PeerId> {
    96	    let (sign_bit, peer_u64, lossless) = peer_id.get_u64();
    97	    if sign_bit {
    98	        return Err(Error::from_reason(
    99	            "peerId BigInt must be non-negative (PeerId is u64-domain)".to_string(),
   100	        ));
   101	    }
   102	    if !lossless {
   103	        return Err(Error::from_reason(
   104	            "peerId BigInt does not fit in u64 (lossy conversion)".to_string(),
   105	        ));
   106	    }
   107	    if peer_u64 == 0 {
   108	        return Err(Error::from_reason(
   109	            "peerId must be non-zero (PeerId(0) is LEGACY_PEER, reserved for pre-collab single-writer + qbook migration)".to_string(),
   110	        ));
   111	    }
   112	    Ok(PeerId::new(peer_u64))
   113	}
   114	
   115	/// JS-facing wrapper for `ql_collab::CollabSession`. See module docs
   116	/// for V1 surface rationale + V2 deferred list.
   117	#[napi]
   118	pub struct CollabSession {
   119	    inner: CoreCollabSession,
   120	}
   121	
   122	#[napi]
   123	impl CollabSession {
   124	    /// Construct a fresh session for the given peer.
   125	    ///
   126	    /// **Failure modes**: `peerId == 0` is rejected by the underlying
   127	    /// engine (PeerId sentinel — Loro reserves the 0 value). napi-rs
   128	    /// surfaces engine errors as JS `Error` exceptions.
   129	    #[napi(constructor)]
   130	    pub fn new(peer_id: BigInt) -> Result<Self> {
   131	        let pid = peer_id_from_bigint(&peer_id)?;
   132	        let session =
   133	            CoreCollabSession::new(pid).map_err(|e| Error::from_reason(format!("{e}")))?;
   134	        Ok(Self { inner: session })
   135	    }
   136	
   137	    /// Reconstruct a session from a previously-exported snapshot.
   138	    /// Mirrors `ql_collab::CollabSession::from_snapshot`.
   139	    #[napi(factory, js_name = "fromSnapshot")]
   140	    pub fn from_snapshot(peer_id: BigInt, bytes: Uint8Array) -> Result<Self> {
   141	        let pid = peer_id_from_bigint(&peer_id)?;
   142	        let session = CoreCollabSession::from_snapshot(pid, bytes.as_ref())
   143	            .map_err(|e| Error::from_reason(format!("{e}")))?;
   144	        Ok(Self { inner: session })
   145	    }
   146	
   147	    /// V1 convenience: append a `PutValue` op with a number value.
   148	    /// The full Op enum binding is V2 work; V1 only exposes this
   149	    /// single variant because it's the minimum that demonstrates
   150	    /// the round-trip.
   151	    #[napi(js_name = "appendPutValue")]
   152	    pub fn append_put_value(&mut self, sheet: u16, row: u32, col: u32, value: f64) -> Result<()> {
   153	        let op = Op::PutValue {
   154	            sheet,
   155	            row,
   156	            col,
   157	            value: CellWireValue::Number(value),
   158	        };
   159	        self.inner
   160	            .append_op(op)
   161	            .map_err(|e| Error::from_reason(format!("{e}")))?;
   162	        Ok(())
   163	    }
   164	
   165	    /// Export a full snapshot of this session's op log.
   166	    /// Mirrors `CollabSession::export_bytes`.
   167	    #[napi(js_name = "exportBytes")]
   168	    pub fn export_bytes(&self) -> Result<Uint8Array> {
   169	        let bytes = self
   170	            .inner
   171	            .export_bytes()
   172	            .map_err(|e| Error::from_reason(format!("{e}")))?;
   173	        Ok(Uint8Array::from(bytes))
   174	    }
   175	
   176	    /// Merge a snapshot (or delta) from another peer.
   177	    /// Returns the number of ops actually merged (Loro dedupes).
   178	    /// Mirrors `CollabSession::merge_bytes`.
   179	    #[napi(js_name = "mergeBytes")]
   180	    pub fn merge_bytes(&mut self, bytes: Uint8Array) -> Result<u32> {
   181	        let count = self
   182	            .inner
   183	            .merge_bytes(bytes.as_ref())
   184	            .map_err(|e| Error::from_reason(format!("{e}")))?;
   185	        Ok(u32::try_from(count).unwrap_or(u32::MAX))
   186	    }
   187	
   188	    /// Local op log length (visible LoroList). See V2 V4 V1 step 2
   189	    /// audit closure for the visible-list-vs-VV distinction; this
   190	    /// method returns the LIST length (matches IDE "how many ops have
   191	    /// I ever logged?" UX). For "how many pending vs last flush,"
   192	    /// use `pendingOpCount()`.
   193	    #[napi(js_name = "opCount")]
   194	    pub fn op_count(&self) -> u32 {
   195	        u32::try_from(self.inner.op_count()).unwrap_or(u32::MAX)
   196	    }
   197	
   198	    /// V2 V4 V1 step 2 helper: count of ops added since the last
   199	    /// successful flush to the currently-attached transport. Uses
   200	    /// VV math (monotonic under undo). Sibling invariant:
   201	    /// `pendingOpCount() > 0` iff `hasPendingFlush() === true`.
   202	    ///
   203	    /// V1 has no transport binding so this returns the count vs
   204	    /// the empty baseline (= same as opCount for a never-flushed
   205	    /// session). V2 will be where this gets interesting once the
   206	    /// Transport surface is bound.
   207	    #[napi(js_name = "pendingOpCount")]
   208	    pub fn pending_op_count(&self) -> u32 {
   209	        u32::try_from(self.inner.pending_op_count()).unwrap_or(u32::MAX)
   210	    }
   211	
   212	    /// V2 V3 step 3 helper: `true` when the local op log has ops
   213	    /// that haven't been flushed to the currently-attached transport.
   214	    #[napi(js_name = "hasPendingFlush")]
   215	    pub fn has_pending_flush(&self) -> bool {
   216	        self.inner.has_pending_flush()
   217	    }
   218	
   219	    /// Peer ID of this session, as a `BigInt` (u64-domain).
   220	    #[napi(js_name = "peerId")]
   221	    pub fn peer_id(&self) -> BigInt {
   222	        BigInt::from(self.inner.peer_id().as_u64())
   223	    }
   224	}
   225	
   226	#[cfg(test)]
   227	mod tests {
   228	    use super::*;
   229	
   230	    #[test]
   231	    fn version_smoke() {
   232	        let v = version();
   233	        assert!(!v.is_empty());
   234	        assert!(v.contains('.'));
   235	    }
   236	
   237	    #[test]
   238	    fn collab_session_roundtrip_via_rust() {
   239	        // Test the Rust side directly (no napi runtime here — that
   240	        // requires a Node host). Validates the wrapper plumbing
   241	        // composes correctly without panics. The full Node-side
   242	        // round-trip lives in the IDE extension's
   243	        // `quantbook-roundtrip.test.ts`.
   244	        let pid_a = PeerId::new(1);
   245	        let mut session_a = CoreCollabSession::new(pid_a).expect("session A");
   246	
   247	        let op = Op::PutValue {
   248	            sheet: 0,
   249	            row: 0,
   250	            col: 0,
   251	            value: CellWireValue::Number(42.0),
   252	        };
   253	        session_a.append_op(op).expect("append");
   254	        assert!(session_a.op_count() >= 1);
   255	
   256	        let bytes = session_a.export_bytes().expect("export");
   257	
   258	        let pid_b = PeerId::new(2);
   259	        let mut session_b = CoreCollabSession::new(pid_b).expect("session B");
   260	        let merged = session_b.merge_bytes(&bytes).expect("merge");

     1	//! `CollabSession` — per-peer collaboration state holder.
     2	//!
     3	//! **Phase 5.2.a (2026-05-19, scaffold ship):** wraps `ql_oplog::OpLog`
     4	//! with peer-id-aware operations + transport plumbing. Subsequent
     5	//! phases populate richer behavior:
     6	//!
     7	//! - ~~**Phase 5.4**~~ ✅ V1 + V2 V1 + V2 V1.1 shipped — V1
     8	//!   `89c02b9d83e`: 7 undo/redo methods. V2 V1 `6138a7203f6` +
     9	//!   `e199a5fda5a`: `start_undo_group` / `end_undo_group` +
    10	//!   `set_undo_merge_interval`. V2 V1.1: `start_undo_group_scoped`
    11	//!   returns RAII `UndoGroupGuard` for panic/Err-safe grouping.
    12	//!   Presence-origin commits auto-excluded.
    13	//! - **Phase 5.5** — transport layer. V1 shipped at
    14	//!   `924750819bc` (LoopbackTransport for in-process 2-peer
    15	//!   tests). V2 V1 shipped at `ffd8f6e5f05` —
    16	//!   `CollabSession::{attach,detach,has}_transport` +
    17	//!   `flush_to_transport` + `poll_remote` (+ `_with_limit`)
    18	//!   typed methods; explicit-drive (caller invokes flush + poll
    19	//!   on a tick). V2 V2 / V3 pending — auto-flush + version-
    20	//!   vector deltas + WebSocket impl.
    21	//! - ~~**Phase 5.6**~~ ✅ V1 + V2 shipped — V1 `c677e244704`:
    22	//!   `presence` module + 4 typed methods. V2: `sweep_presence`
    23	//!   (caller-opt-in clean-slate on rejoin; closes V1 known
    24	//!   persistence limitation).
    25	//! - **Phase 5.7** — IDE binding: `CollabSession` becomes the engine
    26	//!   handle that the IDE attaches to each open workbook.
    27	//!
    28	//! What ships in 5.2.a (5.2.b update 2026-05-19):
    29	//!
    30	//! - `CollabSession::new(peer_id)` — fresh session with an empty
    31	//!   `OpLog` and the peer-id wired through to
    32	//!   `LoroDoc::set_peer_id` (Phase 5.2.b — was a label-only stamp
    33	//!   in 5.2.a). Returns `Result` because Loro's `set_peer_id` is
    34	//!   fallible.
    35	//! - `CollabSession::from_snapshot(peer_id, bytes)` — fork from a
    36	//!   shared base snapshot (used by both peers in the D-4 probe
    37	//!   pattern). The reborn session's peer id is `peer_id`; existing
    38	//!   ops in the imported snapshot retain their original
    39	//!   attribution.
    40	//! - `append_op(&mut self, Op)` — local append; delegates to
    41	//!   `OpLog::append`.
    42	//! - `merge_bytes(&mut self, &[u8])` — pull a remote peer's
    43	//!   snapshot via Loro's CRDT merge; delegates to
    44	//!   `OpLog::merge_bytes`.
    45	//! - `export_bytes(&self)` — produce a snapshot for transport.
    46	//! - `op_log(&self)` — read-only view of the underlying log
    47	//!   (for replay against a workbook).
    48	//! - `peer_id(&self)` — the session's stable peer id.

    63	/// **Phase 5.5 V2 V1 audit closure (2026-05-19):** default cap on

    74	const _ASSERT_COLLAB_SESSION_SEND: fn() = || {

    79	// **V2 V4 V1 step 3 audit closure (Codex L1 + Opus M1, 2026-05-21):**
    80	// CollabSession is Send but NOT Sync — the
    81	// `Option<Box<dyn Transport + Send>>` field carries no Sync bound on
    82	// the trait object, so the field is `?Sync` and the struct inherits
    83	// `!Sync`. This contrasts with `WebSocketTransport` (the concrete
    84	// type), which IS Sync since all its concrete fields happen to be
    85	// Sync. Compile-asserting `!Sync` is awkward in stable Rust;
    86	// documented here via a probe-then-commented-out pattern so a future
    87	// reader can verify the bound by un-commenting and watching the
    88	// build error message naming the !Sync field.

    94	
    95	/// Errors emitted by `CollabSession` operations.
    96	#[derive(Debug, Error)]
    97	#[non_exhaustive]
    98	pub enum CollabSessionError {
    99	    /// The underlying `OpLog` failed (Loro encode/decode, serde,
   100	    /// etc.). Surfaces the wrapped `OpLogError` for diagnosis.
   101	    #[error("op log error: {0}")]
   102	    OpLog(#[from] OpLogError),
   103	
   104	    /// A presence-layer call failed (serde, peer-key parse,
   105	    /// underlying OpLog).
   106	    #[error("presence error: {0}")]
   107	    Presence(#[from] PresenceError),
   108	
   109	    /// **Phase 5.4 V1:** a Loro `UndoManager` call (`undo` /
   110	    /// `redo`) returned an error. Reachable when the underlying
   111	    /// document is in an invalid state for the requested
   112	    /// operation (e.g. mid-transaction).
   113	    #[error("undo/redo error: {0}")]
   114	    Undo(#[from] loro::LoroError),
   115	
   116	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 (2026-05-19):** the attached `Transport`
   117	    /// returned an error.
   118	    ///
   119	    /// For `flush_to_transport`: the local `OpLog` is unaffected
   120	    /// (the snapshot export happened first; the failed step was
   121	    /// `Transport::send`). Caller can retry the flush.
   122	    ///
   123	    /// For `poll_remote`: any bytes successfully drained BEFORE
   124	    /// the error were already `merge_bytes`'d into the local
   125	    /// `OpLog` — those merges persist. The error indicates the
   126	    /// transport itself is misbehaving (`Io`) — `Closed` is
   127	    /// handled gracefully by `poll_remote` itself (returns
   128	    /// `Ok(merged)`, see Codex+Opus 5.5 V2 V1 audit closure).
   129	    /// Caller should detach + reattach a new transport instead
   130	    /// of retrying poll.
   131	    #[error("transport error: {0}")]
   132	    Transport(#[from] TransportError),
   133	
   134	    /// **Phase 5.3 step 5b (2026-05-20) — production wiring closure
   135	    /// (Opus-A Scenario E HIGH).** `rebuild_workbook` invoked
   136	    /// `replay_into` on the session's `OpLog` and replay failed at
   137	    /// some op index. The fresh workbook constructed internally was
   138	    /// in a HALF-MERGED state when the error fired and was DROPPED
   139	    /// before the error was returned (the wrapper owns it). The
   140	    /// session's `OpLog` is unaffected (replay reads only).
   141	    ///
   142	    /// Carries the underlying [`ReplayError`] via `#[source]` so
   143	    /// callers can `match err.source()` for the original op index +
   144	    /// diagnostic.
   145	    #[error("replay error: {0}")]
   146	    Replay(#[from] ReplayError),
   147	}
   148	
   149	/// **Phase 5.3 step 5b (2026-05-20) — production wiring closure
   150	/// (Opus-A Scenario E HIGH):** result of
   151	/// [`CollabSession::rebuild_workbook`]. Combines the replay op count

   153	/// diagnostic surface.
   154	///
   155	/// `#[non_exhaustive]` per Codex step 5b audit LOW closure: column

   159	/// non-breaking.
   160	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
   161	#[non_exhaustive]
   162	pub struct SyncReport {

   176	/// Phase 5.3 step 5b audit closure (Opus MEDIUM-2) + step 5c update:
   177	/// one-line `Display` impl for log-line diagnostic use. Format:
   178	/// `"sync: ops=N sheet_rewrites=N(skip=N) table_rewrites=N(skip=N) column_rewrites=N(skip=N)"`.
   179	impl std::fmt::Display for SyncReport {
   180	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
   181	        write!(
   182	            f,
   183	            "sync: ops={} sheet_rewrites={}(skip={}) table_rewrites={}(skip={}) column_rewrites={}(skip={})",

   187	            self.table_repair.formulas_rewritten,
   188	            self.table_repair.ambiguous_rules_skipped.len(),
   189	            self.column_repair.formulas_rewritten,
   190	            self.column_repair.ambiguous_rules_skipped.len(),
   191	        )
   192	    }
   193	}
   194	
   195	/// **Phase 5.5 V2 V2 (2026-05-21):** auto-flush policy for
   196	/// [`CollabSession`]. Determines when (if ever) the session pushes
   197	/// its current snapshot through the attached transport without an
   198	/// explicit [`CollabSession::flush_to_transport`] call.
   199	///
   200	/// V2 V2 (this ship) adds two variants. V2 V3 will add a `Threshold`
   201	/// variant for byte-budget-based batching.
   202	///
   203	/// `#[non_exhaustive]` so adding a `Threshold(usize)` variant later
   204	/// is non-breaking — callers MUST handle the `_` arm in `match`.
   205	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]
   206	#[non_exhaustive]
   207	pub enum AutoFlushPolicy {
   208	    /// Default. V2 V1 behavior preserved: caller drives flush + poll
   209	    /// explicitly via [`CollabSession::flush_to_transport`] +
   210	    /// [`CollabSession::poll_remote`]. Auto-flush never fires.
   211	    #[default]
   212	    Disabled,
   213	    /// Flush automatically after every public method that mutates the
   214	    /// underlying `LoroDoc`. Triggers on: `append_op`, `merge_bytes`,
   215	    /// `update_presence`, `clear_presence`, `sweep_presence`,
   216	    /// `undo`, `redo`, `poll_remote`, `poll_remote_with_limit`.
   217	    ///
   218	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 1 (2026-05-21):** auto-flush routes
   219	    /// through [`flush_delta_to_transport`] (delta path) rather than
   220	    /// [`flush_to_transport`] (full-snapshot path). Wire payload is
   221	    /// O(per-op delta) instead of O(full state). The idempotency
   222	    /// short-circuit means a flush after no state change is a true
   223	    /// no-op (no transport.send invocation) — this closes the V2 V2
   224	    /// audit echo-loop class.
   225	    ///
   226	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 2 (2026-05-21):** `poll_remote*` now
   227	    /// triggers auto-flush AFTER the drain batch (one flush per
   228	    /// call, not per-blob) — bandwidth-efficient. Reverses the V2 V2
   229	    /// "receive-side excluded by design" exclusion now that V2 V3
   230	    /// step 1's idempotency guard prevents echo loops at the source
   231	    /// (Loro-deduped merge leaves VV unchanged → flush short-circuits
   232	    /// to `Ok(false)`). The 3-peer hub-fanout pattern now works
   233	    /// automatically.
   234	    ///
   235	    /// Does NOT trigger on:
   236	    /// - Accessor methods (read-only).
   237	    /// - Undo-group boundary methods (`start_undo_group` /
   238	    ///   `end_undo_group`) — only their inner appends fire, via
   239	    ///   `append_op`. Each mid-group append produces ONE auto-flush;
   240	    ///   the group is a LOCAL undo unit only (peers see each append
   241	    ///   land independently). Phase 5.5 V2 V2 audit closure (Opus
   242	    ///   L1) — undo-group atomicity-over-wire deferred to V2 V3.
   243	    /// - Transport-lifecycle methods (`attach_transport` etc.).
   244	    ///
   245	    /// If no transport is attached, this is silently a no-op (the
   246	    /// flush hook checks `self.transport.is_some()` first).
   247	    ///
   248	    /// **Partial-state contract on auto-flush error**: if the auto-
   249	    /// flush attempt fails (transport closed, I/O error), the
   250	    /// surrounding mutator (`append_op` etc.) returns
   251	    /// `Err(CollabSessionError::Transport(_))`. The local mutation
   252	    /// has ALREADY been committed to `self.log` (mutate-then-flush
   253	    /// ordering — Loro doesn't support "ungrowing" an op log, V1
   254	    /// limitation H7). Callers MUST treat their local state as
   255	    /// authoritative for the local UI and either retry the flush
   256	    /// (transport recovered) or detach the transport.
   257	    ///
   258	    /// **Symmetric-OnAppend (Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 1 closure,
   259	    /// 2026-05-21):** enabling `OnAppend` on BOTH peers of a paired
   260	    /// transport is safe via the V2 V3 idempotency guard. The
   261	    /// guard short-circuits `flush_delta_to_transport` when no ops
   262	    /// have been appended since the last successful flush. A
   263	    /// merged peer-snapshot that didn't actually advance local
   264	    /// state (Loro CRDT dedupe) leaves the VV unchanged, so the
   265	    /// subsequent auto-flush is a no-op send. The original V2 V2
   266	    /// echo-loop concern is closed at the source. With V2 V3 step 2,
   267	    /// `poll_remote` also auto-flushes — the round-trip back to the
   268	    /// SAME paired transport hits the idempotency guard if the
   269	    /// drained bytes were duplicates, otherwise sends the delta
   270	    /// (peer's Loro dedupes any ops it already has).
   271	    ///
   272	    /// **V1 limit (V2 V3 step 2)**: auto-flush from `poll_remote`
   273	    /// goes back to the SAME transport the bytes came from. In a
   274	    /// true multi-transport hub topology, the merged state would
   275	    /// only fan to ONE transport per session. V2 V3 step 4
   276	    /// (WebSocket + multi-transport routing) may add per-transport
   277	    /// fanout.
   278	    ///
   279	    /// **V1 bandwidth cost — symmetric-OnAppend 2× wire amplification
   280	    /// (V2 V3 step 5 megaudit closure, Opus-B M6, 2026-05-21)**: in a
   281	    /// symmetric 2-peer pairing (A↔B both OnAppend), every appended
   282	    /// op pays a round-trip duplicate wire cost. Trace: A.append →
   283	    /// flush sends to B → B.poll_remote drains → B.maybe_auto_flush
   284	    /// fires (B's VV advanced) → B sends the merged delta (containing
   285	    /// A's op) BACK to A → A.poll_remote drains → A.merge_bytes
   286	    /// Loro-dedupes → A's VV unchanged → A.maybe_auto_flush
   287	    /// idempotency-short-circuits (no further send). The loop
   288	    /// terminates correctly (no echo loop, the V2 V3 step 1 idempotency
   289	    /// guard prevents it), but each op costs 2× wire upload on average.
   290	    /// For multi-peer mesh topologies, the amplification compounds —
   291	    /// V2 V4 per-transport baselines (one `last_flushed_vv` per
   292	    /// attached transport instead of per-session) will close this.
   293	    /// V1-accepted cost; documented in case IDE consumers see "double
   294	    /// bandwidth in network profiler" and need to know it's by design.
   295	    OnAppend,
   296	}
   297	
   298	/// Per-peer collaboration state holder.
   299	///
   300	/// Owns an `OpLog` + a stable `PeerId`. Tested with `NoopTransport`
   301	/// at scaffold time; Phase 5.5 will add the live transport
   302	/// integration via `Transport::send` / `try_recv`.
   303	///
   304	/// The session is the engine-side handle for one peer's view of a
   305	/// shared workbook. Multiple peers each hold their own
   306	/// `CollabSession`, exchange byte blobs via [`crate::Transport`],
   307	/// and call `merge_bytes` to incorporate remote ops. All sessions
   308	/// converge to the same `OpLog` content after exchange (per Loro's
   309	/// CRDT determinism — Phase 5.1 audit Codex V1).
   310	pub struct CollabSession {
   311	    peer_id: PeerId,
   312	    log: OpLog,
   313	    /// Phase 5.4 V1 — peer-local undo/redo.
   314	    ///
   315	    /// Drop-order note (Codex 5.4 V1 audit LOW): Rust drops fields
   316	    /// in DECLARATION order, so this drops LAST (after `log`).
   317	    /// That is safe here because `loro::UndoManager` owns its own
   318	    /// `LoroDoc` clone (`loro-internal::undo:166,670`) — it doesn't
   319	    /// borrow from `log`'s doc and won't UAF when `log` drops first.
   320	    undo: loro::UndoManager,
   321	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V1 (2026-05-19):** optional attached
   322	    /// transport. `None` until `attach_transport` is called.
   323	    /// Boxed + `Send` so callers can move sessions between
   324	    /// threads with attached transports.
   325	    ///
   326	    /// V2 V1 scope was explicit-drive (caller invokes flush + poll
   327	    /// on a tick). V2 V2 (2026-05-21) adds `AutoFlushPolicy::OnAppend`
   328	    /// for IDE callers who want every mutator to propagate.
   329	    transport: Option<Box<dyn Transport + Send>>,
   330	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V2 (2026-05-21):** auto-flush policy. Default
   331	    /// is `Disabled` so existing V2 V1 callers see no behavior
   332	    /// change. Set via [`set_auto_flush_policy`].
   333	    auto_flush_policy: AutoFlushPolicy,
   334	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 1 (2026-05-21):** tracks the
   335	    /// `loro::VersionVector` at the time of the last successful
   336	    /// `flush_to_transport` / `flush_delta_to_transport` call.
   337	    ///
   338	    /// `None` means "no successful flush yet" — the next delta-flush
   339	    /// sends from the empty VV (= all ops the doc has ever seen).
   340	    ///
   341	    /// Lifecycle:
   342	    /// - Initialized `None` in `new` + `from_snapshot`.
   343	    /// - Reset to `None` on `attach_transport` (a new transport-peer
   344	    ///   needs the full state from scratch) and `detach_transport`
   345	    ///   (orphaned VV is meaningless).
   346	    /// - Updated to `Some(current_vv)` after a successful send via
   347	    ///   either `flush_to_transport` (full snapshot path) or
   348	    ///   `flush_delta_to_transport` (V2 V3 delta path).
   349	    /// - NOT updated on flush failure (`Err`) — caller can retry.
   350	    last_flushed_vv: Option<loro::VersionVector>,
   351	}
   352	
   353	impl CollabSession {
   354	    /// Construct a fresh session with an empty op log.
   355	    ///
   356	    /// Use this when no shared base snapshot exists yet (the first
   357	    /// peer to open a brand-new workbook). The `peer_id` is wired
   358	    /// through to the underlying `LoroDoc::set_peer_id` so Loro's
   359	    /// CRDT merge metadata attributes this session's appends to
   360	    /// `peer_id` (not Loro's default random per-doc id).

   626	        Op::RegisterFormat { id, string } => {
   627	            // **W5-80:** route through `FormatTable::register_at` so
   628	            // collisions surface as `FormatRejected` (rather than the
   629	            // panic-on-duplicate behavior `intern` would have via the
   630	            // by_string fast path; `register_at` is the explicit-id form).
   631	            //
   632	            // Phase 5.2 D-1 step 4: `Op::RegisterFormat.id` is now
   633	            // `FormatIdWire`. Convert to `ql_storage::FormatId` via
   634	            // `to_storage()`. The pre-step-4 `legacy_from_u32` migration
   635	            // helper is no longer needed for the wire-replay path; it's
   636	            // reserved for envelope-load migration in step 5.
   637	            let fid = id.to_storage();
   638	            workbook
   639	                .formats_mut()
   640	                .register_at(fid, string.as_str())
   641	                .map_err(|e| ReplayError::FormatRejected {
   642	                    index,
   643	                    source: e.into(),
   644	                })?;
   645	            Ok(())
   646	        }
   647	        Op::SetCellFormat {
   648	            sheet,
   649	            row,
   650	            col,
   651	            id,
   652	        } => {
   653	            validate_cell(workbook, *sheet, *row, *col, index)?;
   654	            // **W5-80:** require the id to be registered. Catches
   655	            // producer bugs where `SetCellFormat` was emitted without
   656	            // a preceding `RegisterFormat`. `None` means clear.
   657	            if let Some(wire_id) = id {
   658	                // Phase 5.2 D-1 step 4: same wire→storage conversion.
   659	                let fid = wire_id.to_storage();
   660	                if workbook.formats().lookup(fid).is_none() {
   661	                    return Err(ReplayError::FormatNotRegistered { index, id: fid });
   662	                }
   663	                workbook
   664	                    .sheet_mut(*sheet)
   665	                    .expect("sheet validated above")
   666	                    .format_overlay_mut()
   667	                    .set(*row, *col, fid);
   668	            } else {
   669	                workbook
   670	                    .sheet_mut(*sheet)
   671	                    .expect("sheet validated above")
   672	                    .format_overlay_mut()
   673	                    .clear(*row, *col);
   674	            }
   675	            Ok(())
   676	        }
   677	        Op::BatchCommit { ops } => {
   678	            for inner_op in ops {
   679	                // Phase 2A.3.a: nested ops flatten to the parent's index
   680	                // for error reporting. Phase 5+ may extend with
   681	                // (outer, inner) tuple indexing once the producer side
   682	                // emits nested commits in practice.
   683	                apply_op(inner_op, workbook, index)?;
   684	            }
   685	            Ok(())
   686	        }
   687	        Op::CreateTable {
   688	            name,
   689	            sheet,
   690	            top_row,
   691	            top_col,
   692	            rows,
   693	            cols,
   694	            has_header,
   695	            has_totals,
   696	            column_names,
   697	        } => apply_create_table(
   698	            workbook,
   699	            index,
   700	            name,
   701	            *sheet,
   702	            *top_row,
   703	            *top_col,
   704	            *rows,
   705	            *cols,
   706	            *has_header,
   707	            *has_totals,
   708	            column_names,
   709	        ),
   710	        Op::DropTable { name } => {
   711	            // **Phase 5.3 step 5 megaudit closure (Codex HIGH + Opus-A
   712	            // V1 LIM #4, 2026-05-20):** advisory-skip on missing source
   713	            // under CRDT merge. Pre-closure the handler hard-failed via
   714	            // `TableNotFound` when the table was already gone, producing
   715	            // an order-dependent failure:
   716	            //   drop(T) then rename(T→T2) → OK (rename advisory-skips)
   717	            //   rename(T→T2) then drop(T) → Err(TableNotFound)
   718	            // The two orderings are logically equivalent (concurrent
   719	            // ops on the same table; final state: table absent), so the
   720	            // hard-fail in one direction is a CRDT-merge regression.
   721	            //
   722	            // Post-closure: idempotent skip when the table is gone. This
   723	            // mirrors the `apply_rename_table` policy at lines 802-820
   724	            // (advisory-skip on missing source) — same bug class, same
   725	            // fix shape.
   726	            let canonical = name.to_ascii_uppercase();
   727	            let _ = workbook.tables_mut().remove(canonical.as_str());
   728	            Ok(())
   729	        }
   730	        Op::RenameTable { old_name, new_name } => {
   731	            apply_rename_table(workbook, index, old_name, new_name)
   732	        }
   733	        Op::RenameColumn {
   734	            table,
   735	            old_name,
   736	            new_name,
   737	        } => apply_rename_column(workbook, index, table, old_name, new_name),
   738	        Op::ResizeTable {
   739	            name,
   740	            new_rows,
   741	            new_cols,
   742	            added_columns,
   743	            removed_columns,
   744	        } => apply_resize_table(
   745	            workbook,
   746	            index,
   747	            name,
   748	            *new_rows,
   749	            *new_cols,
   750	            added_columns,
   751	            removed_columns,
   752	        ),
   753	        // **W5-146 (Phase 4.9.J) + W5-151 (4.9.O MEDIUM-2 closure):**
   754	        // workbook-scope reference-mode change. Forward-compat
   755	        // unknown wire values surface `ReplayError::UnknownReferenceMode`
   756	        // with the captured string (mirrors the `LocaleWire::Unknown`
   757	        // path below).
   758	        Op::SetReferenceMode { mode } => match mode.clone().to_runtime() {
   759	            Ok(m) => {
   760	                workbook.set_reference_mode(m);
   761	                Ok(())
   762	            }
   763	            Err(found) => Err(ReplayError::UnknownReferenceMode { index, found }),
   764	        },
   765	        // **W5-146 (Phase 4.9.J):** workbook-scope locale change.
   766	        // Unknown wire values (forward-compat op-log file) surface
   767	        // `ReplayError::UnknownLocale` with the captured string.
   768	        Op::SetLocale { locale } => match locale.clone().to_runtime() {
   769	            Ok(loc) => {
   770	                workbook.set_locale(loc);
   771	                Ok(())
   772	            }
   773	            Err(found) => Err(ReplayError::UnknownLocale { index, found }),
   774	        },
   775	    }
   776	}
   777	
   778	/// **W5-119 (Phase 4.8.I) + Phase 5.3 step 4 (2026-05-20):**
   779	/// replay a `RenameTable`. Validates source exists, target name is
   780	/// available (TableTable + NameTable shared namespace), then re-keys
   781	/// the entry.
   782	///
   783	/// **Phase 5.3 step 4 (audit-locked, mirrors step 2 for
   784	/// RenameSheet):** two CRDT-merge scenarios required this handler to
   785	/// be made resilient:
   786	///
   787	/// 1. **Concurrent rename of same table** (last-wins): peer A
   788	///    renames T→T1, peer B (concurrent) renames T→T2. After Loro's
   789	///    causal merge, the second op's `old_name="T"` no longer
   790	///    resolves to a live table. Pre-step-4 this errored with
   791	///    `TableNotFound`. Post-step-4: if NO table at `old_canonical`
   792	///    exists but the workbook still has the post-first-rename
   793	///    name (T1 or T2 depending on causal order), we no-op the
   794	///    second rename if its `new_name` matches a current table
   795	///    (idempotency); otherwise, we treat the rename as advisory
   796	///    and skip if the table simply doesn't exist anywhere.
   797	///    Note: unlike sheets which carry stable IDs, tables are
   798	///    keyed by canonical name — there's no equivalent "rename
   799	///    current table to new_name" because we can't disambiguate
   800	///    which current table the wire op was thought to be acting on.
   801	///    Skip is the safer policy.
   802	///
   803	/// 2. **Cross-table target collision** (V1 LIMITATION — hard-fail):
   804	///    peer A renames T1→X, peer B renames T2→X (different sources,
   805	///    same target). After merge, the second op errors with
   806	///    `TableCreateRejected`. Pre-step-4-audit the handler attempted
   807	///    D-2-style auto-disambig (X → X(2)), but the step 4 audit
   808	///    (Codex+Opus HIGH-1) showed this produced silent formula-
   809	///    corruption when combined with `repair_table_rename_chain` —
   810	///    the repair keyed its chain by op's wire `new_name` and missed
   811	///    the auto-disambig'd canonical. Reverted to hard-reject. V2
   812	///    closure path: emit synthesized correction op at replay (would
   813	///    require API change to surface the disambig outcome to repair).
   814	fn apply_rename_table(
   815	    workbook: &mut Workbook,
   816	    index: usize,
   817	    old_name: &str,
   818	    new_name: &str,
   819	) -> Result<(), ReplayError> {
   820	    let old_canonical = old_name.to_ascii_uppercase();
   821	
   822	    // **Step 4 audit-locked policy** (V1 — tables): source table
   823	    // missing under CRDT merge means a concurrent peer renamed it.
   824	    // Three scenarios:
   825	    //  (a) the rename has already been applied as part of this peer's
   826	    //      causal-order replay (idempotent — current state has new_name).
   827	    //  (b) a concurrent peer renamed it to a DIFFERENT target (e.g.
   828	    //      peer A: T→T2; peer B: T→T3). Sheets handle this via stable
   829	    //      sheet IDs (rename current sheet to new_name); tables don't
   830	    //      have stable IDs (keyed by canonical name) so we can't
   831	    //      disambiguate which table this op was thought to be acting
   832	    //      on. Apply "advisory skip" — drop the second rename's intent
   833	    //      to avoid corruption.
   834	    //  (c) the source genuinely doesn't exist (corruption or stale op
   835	    //      against a never-existed table).
   836	    //
   837	    // V1 policy: skip silently in case (a) and (b); error only when
   838	    // there's clearly no related table state to interpret the op
   839	    // against (no current_canonical, no new_canonical, no plausible
   840	    // post-rename state).
   841	    //
   842	    // **V1 limitation (deferred to V2):** scenario (b) loses peer
   843	    // B's rename intent. Causality-aware tracking would let us tell
   844	    // "this op was authored when T existed locally; the table was
   845	    // since renamed to T2 by a concurrent peer; we COULD apply
   846	    // T2→T3." Today the only safe call is advisory-skip.
   847	    if workbook.tables().lookup(&old_canonical).is_none() {
   848	        let new_canonical = new_name.to_ascii_uppercase();
   849	        if workbook.tables().lookup(&new_canonical).is_some() {
   850	            // Case (a): idempotent — new_name already exists.
   851	            return Ok(());
   852	        }
   853	        // Cases (b) and (c): no related state. Advisory skip
   854	        // (case b) accepts losing the rename intent rather than
   855	        // hard-failing replay; the alternative would be re-erroring
   856	        // and making merged logs un-replayable. The diagnostic
   857	        // surface for (b) would require op-log-level reporting
   858	        // beyond replay's return type — deferred to V2.
   859	        return Ok(());
   860	    }
   861	
   862	    // **Step 4 audit closure (Codex+Opus HIGH-1, 2026-05-20):**
   863	    // auto-disambiguation REVERTED. The previous implementation
   864	    // walked suffixes (X → X(2)) on TableTable collision, but the
   865	    // repair pass at `ql_collab::repair_table_rename_chain` keys
   866	    // its chain by the op's wire `new_name`, not the actual
   867	    // post-replay canonical (which would be the suffixed `X(2)`).
   868	    // That mismatch caused silent formula-text corruption:
   869	    // formulas referencing peer B's old table got rewritten to the
   870	    // OTHER table named X (peer A's), pointing at the wrong sheet.
   871	    //
   872	    // Reverting to hard-reject on collision restores correctness:
   873	    // - Cross-source target collision (peer A: T1→X; peer B: T2→X
   874	    //   concurrent): the second op errors via TableCreateRejected.
   875	    //   This is a HARD-FAIL — V1 limitation, documented in plan.
   876	    //   Future V2 closure paths: emit synthesized correction op at
   877	    //   replay (would require API change), OR reconcile via
   878	    //   causality-aware tracking.
   879	    let new_canonical_arc: std::sync::Arc<str> =
   880	        std::sync::Arc::from(new_name.to_ascii_uppercase().as_str());
   881	    // No-op same-canonical case.
   882	    if old_canonical.eq_ignore_ascii_case(new_name) {
   883	        return Ok(());
   884	    }
   885	    // NameTable collision (different namespace).
   886	    if workbook.names().lookup_ci(&new_canonical_arc).is_some() {
   887	        return Err(ReplayError::TableCreateRejected {
   888	            index,
   889	            name: new_name.to_owned(),
   890	            reason: "defined-name with this canonical name already exists (rename target)",
   891	        });
   892	    }
   893	    // TableTable collision.
   894	    if workbook.tables().lookup(&new_canonical_arc).is_some() {
   895	        return Err(ReplayError::TableCreateRejected {
   896	            index,
   897	            name: new_name.to_owned(),
   898	            reason: "table with this canonical name already exists (rename target)",
   899	        });
   900	    }
   901	    // Apply the rename.
   902	    let mut meta = workbook
   903	        .tables_mut()
   904	        .remove(&old_canonical)

     1	//! `OpLog` — Loro-backed append-only operation log.
     2	//!
     3	//! Phase 2A.3.a (2026-05-12): scaffolding-only API. The log stores ops as
     4	//! JSON-encoded strings inside a single Loro `LoroList` container named
     5	//! `"ops"`. Each `OpLog::append` call serializes the `Op`, pushes a string
     6	//! into the list, and commits the Loro doc.
     7	//!
     8	//! ## Why JSON-in-Loro
     9	//!
    10	//! Per Round 7 architectural lock T1-D05, Loro is reserved for the op log
    11	//! layer. Phase 2A.3.a aims for minimum viable scaffolding — full CRDT
    12	//! semantics (LoroMap-per-op with typed fields) is reserved for Phase 5+
    13	//! when inter-peer merging becomes load-bearing. The JSON layer is
    14	//! invisible to callers: they see typed `Op` values via `iter`.
    15	//!
    16	//! ## Persistence
    17	//!
    18	//! `export_bytes` produces a `Vec<u8>` via `ExportMode::Snapshot`. The
    19	//! resulting blob includes the full state plus history; **suitable for
    20	//! transport to other peers** (consumed by `merge_bytes` on the
    21	//! receiver). For `.qbook/oplog.bin` file persistence, the
    22	//! `ql_io::oplog_persistence` layer wraps this blob in a Quantlab
    23	//! header (Phase 5.2 D-1 step 7 — Tier D3: `OPLOG_MAGIC` + version u32).
    24	//! Do NOT write `export_bytes()` output directly to `oplog.bin`;
    25	//! use `save_workbook_with_oplog` instead.
    26	//!
    27	//! `import_bytes` reconstructs an `OpLog` from such a blob (raw Loro
    28	//! snapshot — the persistence layer strips the Quantlab header before
    29	//! calling this fn). The reader probes the `"ops"` LoroList; if absent
    30	//! (or the wrong shape), the import returns `OpLogError::SchemaMismatch`.
    31	
    32	use loro::{CommitOptions, ExportMode, LoroDoc, LoroList, LoroValue, ValueOrContainer};
    33	
    34	use crate::error::OpLogError;
    35	use crate::op::Op;
    36	
    37	/// The Loro container name our ops live under. Hard-coded; Phase 5+
    38	/// CRDT-level refactoring may add more containers (versions, peer state,
    39	/// etc.) but `"ops"` is reserved.
    40	const OPS_CONTAINER: &str = "ops";
    41	
    42	/// **Phase 5.6 (2026-05-19):** Loro container name for the per-peer
    43	/// ephemeral presence map (cursor + selection + typing indicator).
    44	/// Per the audit-closed Phase 5.1 design at
    45	/// `docs/architecture/crdt-data-model.md` § Presence container.
    46	/// Keys are peer-id strings; values are opaque JSON blobs (the
    47	/// typed `PresenceState` encoding lives in `ql_collab::presence`).
    48	const PRESENCE_CONTAINER: &str = "presence";
    49	
    50	/// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** commit-origin tag for presence
    51	/// writes. `ql_collab::CollabSession` wires this to Loro's
    52	/// `UndoManager::add_exclude_origin_prefix` so cursor movements
    53	/// don't pollute the undo stack. Exported as `pub` so the higher-
    54	/// layer (ql-collab) can reference the same string constant.
    55	///
    56	/// **Reserved-namespace convention:** trailing `":"` so the prefix
    57	/// `presence:` only matches deliberately-namespaced origins (e.g.
    58	/// a future `presence:typing` would also be excluded; an
    59	/// independent `"presence-foo"` origin would NOT). This protects
    60	/// against accidental over-exclusion as more origins land
    61	/// (Codex+Opus 5.4 V1 audit findings A4/M5).
    62	pub const PRESENCE_COMMIT_ORIGIN: &str = "presence:";
    63	
    64	/// Append-only operation log backed by Loro.
    65	///
    66	/// Construct via `OpLog::new()` for a fresh log or `OpLog::import_bytes(...)`
    67	/// to reconstruct from a persisted snapshot. Mutations go through `append`;
    68	/// reads through `iter` / `len` / `is_empty`.
    69	pub struct OpLog {
    70	    doc: LoroDoc,
    71	}
    72	
    73	impl std::fmt::Debug for OpLog {
    74	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
    75	        f.debug_struct("OpLog")
    76	            .field("len", &self.len())
    77	            .field("doc", &"<LoroDoc>")
    78	            .finish()
    79	    }
    80	}
    81	
    82	impl Default for OpLog {
    83	    fn default() -> Self {
    84	        Self::new()
    85	    }
    86	}
    87	
    88	impl OpLog {
    89	    /// Construct an empty log.
    90	    pub fn new() -> Self {
    91	        Self {
    92	            doc: LoroDoc::new(),
    93	        }
    94	    }
    95	
    96	    /// Append one `Op` to the log. Serializes via `serde_json`, stores as
    97	    /// a `LoroValue::String` in the `"ops"` LoroList, and commits the
    98	    /// underlying Loro doc.
    99	    pub fn append(&mut self, op: Op) -> Result<(), OpLogError> {
   100	        let json = serde_json::to_string(&op).map_err(OpLogError::Serialize)?;
   101	        let list: LoroList = self.doc.get_list(OPS_CONTAINER);
   102	        list.push(LoroValue::from(json.as_str()))?;
   103	        self.doc.commit();
   104	        Ok(())
   105	    }
   106	
   107	    /// Iterate the log in append order. Each yielded item is a
   108	    /// `Result<Op, OpLogError>` — a deserialization failure yields
   109	    /// `Err(OpLogError::Deserialize { index, source })` and iteration
   110	    /// continues (no short-circuit). Callers wanting fail-fast can use
   111	    /// `iter().collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()`.
   112	    pub fn iter(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = Result<Op, OpLogError>> + '_ {
   113	        let list: LoroList = self.doc.get_list(OPS_CONTAINER);
   114	        let len = list.len();
   115	        (0..len).map(move |index| {
   116	            let entry = list.get(index).ok_or(OpLogError::SchemaMismatch(
   117	                "ops LoroList lost an entry between len() and get()",
   118	            ))?;
   119	            let value = match entry {
   120	                ValueOrContainer::Value(v) => v,
   121	                ValueOrContainer::Container(_) => {
   122	                    return Err(OpLogError::SchemaMismatch(
   123	                        "ops LoroList holds a container; expected JSON-string values",
   124	                    ));
   125	                }
   126	            };
   127	            let s = match value {
   128	                LoroValue::String(s) => s,
   129	                _ => {
   130	                    return Err(OpLogError::SchemaMismatch(
   131	                        "ops LoroList holds a non-string LoroValue; expected JSON-string",
   132	                    ));
   133	                }
   134	            };
   135	            serde_json::from_str::<Op>(&s)
   136	                .map_err(|source| OpLogError::Deserialize { index, source })
   137	        })
   138	    }
   139	
   140	    /// Number of ops currently visible in the `"ops"` LoroList.
   141	    ///
   142	    /// **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** queries Loro directly each call
   143	    /// (was a cached `usize` field through 5.6). Codex 5.4 V1 audit
   144	    /// HIGH found the cache became stale after `loro::UndoManager`
   145	    /// retracted ops from the visible list. Returning Loro's
   146	    /// authoritative count is correct by construction; per-call
   147	    /// cost is one container handle lookup + a length read.
   148	    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
   149	        self.doc.get_list(OPS_CONTAINER).len()
   150	    }
   151	
   152	    /// True iff `len() == 0`.
   153	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
   154	        self.len() == 0
   155	    }
   156	
   157	    /// **Phase 5.2.b (2026-05-19):** set this log's Loro peer id.
   158	    ///
   159	    /// Wraps `LoroDoc::set_peer_id`. Used by `ql_collab::CollabSession`
   160	    /// to wire its stable `PeerId` through to Loro so concurrent
   161	    /// appends carry the right origin in the CRDT merge metadata.
   162	    ///
   163	    /// Loro takes `&self` (interior mutability), so this method is
   164	    /// `&self` too. Calling it twice with the same id leaves the
   165	    /// peer id unchanged but is NOT a strict no-op at Loro's
   166	    /// internal layer: Loro re-stores the atom and emits a
   167	    /// `peer_id_change_subs` notification each call. Avoid calling
   168	    /// repeatedly in hot paths.
   169	    ///
   170	    /// **Caller pitfalls** (from `loro::LoroDoc::set_peer_id`):
   171	    /// 1. NEVER reuse the same peer id across concurrent writers
   172	    ///    (multiple tabs / devices for the same user). Duplicate peer
   173	    ///    ids corrupt the document via conflicting OpIDs.
   174	    /// 2. Avoid pinning a peer id to a stable user/device identity
   175	    ///    unless you also enforce single-ownership locking. Prefer a
   176	    ///    per-process-random peer id.
   177	    /// 3. Setting peer id AFTER an import is safe — existing imported
   178	    ///    ops retain their original peer ids; only this log's future
   179	    ///    appends use the new id.
   180	    /// 4. Loro reserves `u64::MAX` as a sentinel; passing it returns
   181	    ///    `OpLogError::Loro(LoroError::InvalidPeerID)`.
   182	    /// 5. **Phase 5.4 V1 (2026-05-19):** if a `loro::UndoManager`
   183	    ///    (constructed via [`new_undo_manager`]) is alive, Loro
   184	    ///    SILENTLY CLEARS its undo + redo stacks on the peer-id

   186	    ///    treat post-construction peer-id changes as undo-stack-
   187	    ///    invalidating events.
   188	    ///
   189	    /// Signature note: `&mut self` (Phase 5.4 V1 audit closure)
   190	    /// even though Loro's `LoroDoc::set_peer_id` is `&self`. The
   191	    /// `&mut` prevents accidental peer-id changes through a shared
   192	    /// `&OpLog` (`CollabSession::op_log()`) which would silently
   193	    /// clear an attached `UndoManager`'s stacks per pitfall 5.
   194	    ///
   195	    /// **Phase 5.2 D-1 step 8 megaudit closure (Opus-B HIGH-1,
   196	    /// 2026-05-20):** asserts `peer != 0`. PeerId(0) is `LEGACY_PEER`
   197	    /// — reserved as the sentinel for pre-collab single-writer +
   198	    /// qbook envelope migration. An active session whose Loro peer
   199	    /// id is 0 would silently collide with the LEGACY_PEER semantic
   200	    /// in `FormatTable::with_peer(LEGACY_PEER)` (the cross-peer
   201	    /// same-string design assumes LEGACY_PEER is sentinel-only).
   202	    /// `CollabSession::new` + `from_snapshot` already assert this at
   203	    /// the higher level; this guard catches direct callers of
   204	    /// `OpLog::set_peer_id` that bypass the CollabSession constructor.
   205	    pub fn set_peer_id(&mut self, peer: u64) -> Result<(), OpLogError> {
   206	        assert_ne!(
   207	            peer, 0,
   208	            "PeerId(0) is LEGACY_PEER (reserved for pre-collab single-writer + qbook migration). \
   209	             OpLog::set_peer_id must be called with a non-zero peer; collides with LEGACY_PEER otherwise."
   210	        );
   211	        self.doc.set_peer_id(peer)?;
   212	        Ok(())
   213	    }

   220	    /// **Phase 5.6 (2026-05-19):** write the given peer's presence blob

   362	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V4 V1 step 5 (2026-05-21) — Tier I2.** Fork
   363	    /// the underlying `LoroDoc` at the given VersionVector, returning
   364	    /// a new `OpLog` whose history ends at that VV. Used by
   365	    /// [`ql_collab::CollabSession::discard_pending_ops`] to revert
   366	    /// pending ops back to the last-flushed checkpoint.
   367	    ///
   368	    /// Composes `LoroDoc::vv_to_frontiers` + `LoroDoc::fork_at`:
   369	    /// 1. Convert `vv` to the precise causal-history Frontiers marker.
   370	    /// 2. Fork the doc — the new doc contains ONLY ops with
   371	    ///    counter ≤ the corresponding entry in `vv`, per peer.
   372	    ///
   373	    /// The returned `OpLog`'s `oplog_vv()` matches the input `vv`
   374	    /// exactly. The peer-id of the new doc is fresh (Loro's default
   375	    /// at construction time); callers should `set_peer_id` if they
   376	    /// want to preserve their stable PeerId for subsequent appends.
   377	    ///
   378	    /// # Errors
   379	    ///
   380	    /// - `OpLogError::InvalidVersionVector(_)` if `vv` is ahead of
   381	    ///   the current `oplog_vv` (per-peer counter exceeds the local)
   382	    ///   or references peers not in the local history. **V2 V4 V1
   383	    ///   step 5 audit closure (Codex M1, 2026-05-21):** Loro 1.12's
   384	    ///   `vv_to_frontiers` internally `.unwrap()`s in these cases
   385	    ///   and would panic; pre-validate at the OpLog API boundary
   386	    ///   and surface as a proper Err.
   387	    /// - `OpLogError::Loro(_)` if `LoroDoc::fork_at` fails post-
   388	    ///   validation (should not happen if validation passes, but
   389	    ///   defensive `?` propagation).
   390	    pub fn fork_at_vv(&self, vv: &loro::VersionVector) -> Result<Self, OpLogError> {
   391	        // V2 V4 V1 step 5 audit closure (Codex M1): pre-validate
   392	        // the VV against our current oplog_vv. Reject if vv has any
   393	        // peer with a counter greater than ours (vv is ahead) OR
   394	        // references a peer we've never seen.
   395	        let current = self.doc.oplog_vv();
   396	        for (peer, vv_counter) in vv.iter() {
   397	            let current_counter = current.get(peer).copied().unwrap_or(0);
   398	            if *vv_counter > current_counter {
   399	                return Err(OpLogError::InvalidVersionVector(format!(
   400	                    "VV references peer {peer:?} with counter {vv_counter} > current {current_counter}; \
   401	                     cannot fork ahead of local history"
   402	                )));
   403	            }
   404	        }
   405	        let frontiers = self.doc.vv_to_frontiers(vv);
   406	        let forked = self.doc.fork_at(&frontiers)?;
   407	        Ok(Self { doc: forked })
   408	    }
   409	
   410	    /// Reconstruct an `OpLog` from a previously-exported snapshot.
   411	    /// If the snapshot doesn't carry our `"ops"` container shape,
   412	    /// subsequent `iter` / `len` calls operate on an empty list
   413	    /// (Loro creates absent root containers on first access).
   414	    pub fn import_bytes(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Self, OpLogError> {
   415	        let doc = LoroDoc::new();
   416	        doc.import(bytes)?;
   417	        Ok(Self { doc })
   418	    }
   419	
   420	    /// **Phase 5.2 D-4 (2026-05-19):** merge another peer's snapshot
   421	    /// into this log via Loro's CRDT merge. Concurrent appends to
   422	    /// the `"ops"` LoroList are preserved in deterministic causal
   423	    /// order (Fugue/origin-based with peer-id tiebreaker per
   424	    /// `loro-internal::container::richtext::tracker::crdt_rope`).
   425	    ///
   426	    /// Typical pattern for multi-peer collaboration:
   427	    /// 1. Both peers start from a shared snapshot (`import_bytes`).
   428	    /// 2. Each peer appends its own ops independently.
   429	    /// 3. Peer A exports its current state via `export_bytes`.
   430	    /// 4. Peer B calls `merge_bytes(&a_bytes)` — peer B's log now
   431	    ///    contains both peers' ops in causal order.
   432	    /// 5. Replay against a workbook reconstructs the merged state.
   433	    /// 6. `WorkbookRuntime::recompute_all` resolves derived state
   434	    ///    (spills, formula values) from the merged final state.
   435	    ///
   436	    /// Returns the new `len()` after merge.
   437	    pub fn merge_bytes(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<usize, OpLogError> {
   438	        self.doc.import(bytes)?;
   439	        Ok(self.len())
   440	    }

  1257	    pub fn flush_delta_to_transport(&mut self) -> Result<bool, CollabSessionError> {
  1258	        if self.transport.is_none() {

  1266	        // appended/merged anything since the prior flush returns
  1267	        // Ok(false) without invoking transport.send.

  1271	            }
  1272	        }

   624	        }
   625	        let ops_replayed = replay_into(&self.log, &mut workbook, registry)?;
   626	        let sheet_repair = repair_sheet_rename_chain(&mut workbook, &self.log)?;
   627	        let table_repair = repair_table_rename_chain(&mut workbook, &self.log)?;
   628	        let column_repair = repair_column_rename_chain(&mut workbook, &self.log)?;
   629	        Ok((
   630	            workbook,
   631	            SyncReport {
   632	                ops_replayed,
   633	                sheet_repair,
   634	                table_repair,
   635	                column_repair,
   636	            },
   637	        ))
   638	    }

   653	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V1 (2026-05-19):** attach a `Transport`

   673	    /// seen any of this session's ops. The next
   674	    /// [`flush_delta_to_transport`] (or any auto-flushing mutator)
   675	    /// sends from the empty VV — i.e., ALL local ops including
   676	    /// those appended while offline. **Loro's CRDT op log IS the
   677	    /// offline queue; no separate buffer needed.**
   678	    ///
   679	    /// **Idiom for explicit reattach-then-sync** (recommended for
   680	    /// callers that want the new peer to see all state
   681	    /// immediately, rather than waiting for the next mutator):
   682	    /// ```ignore

   684	    /// if session.has_pending_flush() {

   686	    /// }

   689	    /// lifecycle event, not a mutation, and the existing API
   690	    /// returns `Option<previous>` rather than `Result<>` (changing
   691	    /// that would be a breaking V2 V1 API change). Callers needing
   692	    /// immediate sync trigger it explicitly.
   693	    pub fn attach_transport<T: Transport + Send + 'static>(
   694	        &mut self,
   695	        transport: T,
   696	    ) -> Option<Box<dyn Transport + Send>> {
   697	        // **Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 1 (2026-05-21):** reset `last_flushed_vv`
   698	        // to `None`. A new transport-peer hasn't seen ANY of this
   699	        // session's ops, so the next `flush_delta_to_transport` must
   700	        // send from the empty VV (= all ops). If we kept the stale
   701	        // VV from a prior transport, the new peer would miss ops
   702	        // 0..stale_vv and end up with a corrupt view.
   703	        self.last_flushed_vv = None;
