   860	    /// 1. Brand-new `CollabSession::new(...)` with no ops + no
   861	    ///    flushes — `current_vv = default` matches
   862	    ///    `last_flushed_vv.unwrap_or_default() = default`.
   863	    /// 2. All ops flushed — `last_flushed_vv = Some(current_vv)`.
   864	    /// 3. Brand-new session AFTER `detach_transport` AND no further
   865	    ///    mutations — `current_vv = default` matches `default`.
   866	    ///
   867	    /// Three scenarios that return `true` even though no transport
   868	    /// is attached:
   869	    /// 1. Brand-new session with offline appends (no transport
   870	    ///    ever attached) — `current_vv` reflects local ops;
   871	    ///    `last_flushed_vv = None` → `default`.
   872	    /// 2. `CollabSession::from_snapshot(peer_id, bytes)` IMMEDIATELY
   873	    ///    after construction — `current_vv` reflects the IMPORTED
   874	    ///    ops (typically non-empty); `last_flushed_vv = None`.
   875	    ///    Callers building IDE "Synced / Unsynced" indicators
   876	    ///    should either suppress until first user-driven mutation
   877	    ///    OR wrap with `has_transport() && has_pending_flush()`
   878	    ///    to gate the indicator on the transport-attached state.
   879	    /// 3. Post-`detach_transport` non-empty session — the orphaned
   880	    ///    `last_flushed_vv` was reset to `None`; `current_vv` is
   881	    ///    still non-empty.
   882	    ///
   883	    /// Bottom line: combine with [`has_transport`] when the IDE
   884	    /// status should reflect "do we have an active sync path"
   885	    /// rather than just "is there local state."
   886	    ///
   887	    /// # Offline-write story (Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 3)
   888	    ///
   889	    /// Append while no transport attached: `append_op` succeeds
   890	    /// (V2 V2 contract — `maybe_auto_flush` returns `Ok(())`
   891	    /// silently when no transport). The op is committed locally;
   892	    /// `has_pending_flush()` returns `true`. On
   893	    /// [`attach_transport`], `last_flushed_vv` resets to `None`.
   894	    /// The next mutator (or explicit `flush_delta_to_transport`)
   895	    /// sends the delta from empty VV — i.e., ALL accumulated ops
   896	    /// including the offline ones. Loro's CRDT op log IS the
   897	    /// offline queue.
   898	    pub fn has_pending_flush(&self) -> bool {

   900	        let last = self.last_flushed_vv.clone().unwrap_or_default();

   902	    }

   904	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V4 V1 step 2 (2026-05-21) — Tier I1.** Count of
   905	    /// Loro causal-history entries (per-peer counter deltas) added to
   906	    /// the local op log since the last successful flush to the
   907	    /// currently-attached transport. Sibling to [`has_pending_flush`] —
   908	    /// same underlying invariant (`current_vv != last_flushed_vv`),
   909	    /// finer-grained observable (returns the **magnitude** of the
   910	    /// difference rather than just a boolean).
   911	    ///
   912	    /// # Implementation
   913	    ///
   914	    /// Computed by VV math: for each peer in `current_vv`, take the
   915	    /// per-peer counter delta from the corresponding entry in
   916	    /// `last_flushed_vv` (or 0 if absent), and sum the positive
   917	    /// deltas. VV counters are monotonic per peer in Loro's causal
   918	    /// history — they only INCREASE within a single peer's lifetime
   919	    /// (each new op the peer authors gets the next counter value).
   920	    /// This makes the VV-based count strictly monotonic under all
   921	    /// existing log mutations including `undo` (which Loro records as
   922	    /// a new causal-history entry that advances the VV even though it
   923	    /// retracts a visible op from the LoroList).
   924	    ///
   925	    /// **V2 V4 V1 step 2 audit closure (Codex M1 / Opus H1,
   926	    /// 2026-05-21):** the initial ship used `self.log.len()` (visible
   927	    /// LoroList length) as the count source. That's NOT monotonic
   928	    /// under undo — Loro's `UndoManager` retracts ops from the visible
   929	    /// list, so `log.len()` can shrink while `current_vv` still
   930	    /// advances. Result: `pending_op_count() == 0` while
   931	    /// `has_pending_flush() == true` — divergent observability that
   932	    /// breaks the documented sibling relationship. The convergent
   933	    /// audit finding caught this; the closure switched to VV math,
   934	    /// which is monotonic under undo (the undo's inverse op is a new
   935	    /// VV entry).
   936	    ///
   937	    /// # IDE consumer use cases
   938	    ///
   939	    /// - **Bounded-queue policy**: "if `pending_op_count() > 1000`,
   940	    ///   switch the editor to read-only mode until reconnect."
   941	    /// - **Status indicator gradient**: "🟢 Synced if 0, 🟡 N pending
   942	    ///   if 1..50, 🟠 backlog warning if >50."
   943	    /// - **Memory pressure rough estimate**: each VV entry maps to one
   944	    ///   Loro op (causal-history-wise); `pending_op_count() *
   945	    ///   size_of_avg_op` is a memory floor for the not-yet-delivered
   946	    ///   delta blob.
   947	    ///
   948	    /// # Semantic note — count includes peer ops AND undo ops
   949	    ///
   950	    /// The returned count is the magnitude of "VV entries the
   951	    /// currently-attached transport hasn't seen," NOT "user's own
   952	    /// unsynced edits." Includes BOTH local appends AND ops merged
   953	    /// from peers since the last flush. **Includes undo ops** — each
   954	    /// undo is a new causal-history entry (even though it retracts a
   955	    /// visible op). For finer-grained "my net visible edits" an IDE
   956	    /// builds its own peer-id-filtered counter — out of V2 V4 V1
   957	    /// scope.
   958	    ///
   959	    /// # Edge cases
   960	    ///
   961	    /// - Fresh `CollabSession::new` session, no ops, no transport:
   962	    ///   returns `0` (`current_vv == default`, `last_flushed_vv` is
   963	    ///   `None.unwrap_or_default() = default`).
   964	    /// - `from_snapshot` session: returns the imported VV's total
   965	    ///   counter sum immediately, even before `attach_transport`.
   966	    ///   Matches `has_pending_flush() == true` for the same scenario.
   967	    /// - Post-`attach_transport`: baseline reset to `None`; count
   968	    ///   includes ALL local ops (matches V2 V3 step 1 contract that
   969	    ///   next flush sends from empty VV).
   970	    /// - Post-successful-flush: baseline at `current_vv`; count `0`
   971	    ///   (paired with `has_pending_flush() == false`).
   972	    /// - Post-undo (with disabled auto-flush or no working transport):
   973	    ///   count GROWS by 1 (the undo's inverse op is a new VV entry);
   974	    ///   `has_pending_flush() == true`. The sibling relationship
   975	    ///   `pending_op_count() > 0 ⟺ has_pending_flush() == true` holds
   976	    ///   for all current mutators.
   977	    pub fn pending_op_count(&self) -> usize {
   978	        let current = self.log.oplog_vv();
   979	        let last = self.last_flushed_vv.clone().unwrap_or_default();
   980	        // Sum positive per-peer counter deltas. VV counters are
   981	        // monotonic per peer in Loro's causal history, so
   982	        // current[peer] >= last[peer] always holds; we use
   983	        // `saturating_sub` as defensive insurance.
   984	        let mut total: u64 = 0;
   985	        for (peer, current_counter) in current.iter() {
   986	            let last_counter = last.get(peer).copied().unwrap_or(0);
   987	            let diff = (*current_counter as i64)
   988	                .saturating_sub(last_counter as i64)
   989	                .max(0) as u64;
   990	            total = total.saturating_add(diff);
   991	        }
   992	        total as usize
   993	    }

    21	/**
    22	 * A peer-to-peer collaboration session. Wraps the engine's
    23	 * `ql_collab::CollabSession`.
    24	 *
    25	 * **Failure modes** (constructors throw JS `Error`):
    26	 * - `peerId == 0n` -- PeerId reserves 0 (Loro sentinel)
    27	 * - `peerId < 0n` -- PeerId is u64-domain
    28	 * - `peerId` exceeds u64 -- lossy BigInt conversion
    29	 *
    30	 * Method failures (engine-level errors, e.g., merge of malformed
    31	 * bytes) surface as JS `Error` exceptions with the engine's error
    32	 * message string.
    33	 */
    34	export interface CollabSessionInstance {
    35		/**
    36		 * V1 convenience: append a `PutValue` op with a numeric value.
    37		 * Full Op enum binding is V2 work; V1 exposes this single variant
    38		 * because it's the minimum that demonstrates the round-trip.
    39		 */
    40		appendPutValue(sheet: number, row: number, col: number, value: number): void;
    41	
    42		/** Full snapshot export. Use for initial sync / handshake. */
    43		exportBytes(): Uint8Array;
    44	
    45		/**
    46		 * Merge a snapshot (or delta) from another peer. Returns the count
    47		 * of ops actually merged (Loro dedupes by causal history).
    48		 */
    49		mergeBytes(bytes: Uint8Array): number;
    50	
    51		/** Local op log length (visible LoroList). */
    52		opCount(): number;
    53	
    54		/**
    55		 * V2 V4 V1 step 2 helper: count of ops added since the last
    56		 * successful flush. Uses VV math (monotonic under undo). Sibling
    57		 * invariant: `pendingOpCount() > 0` iff `hasPendingFlush() === true`.
    58		 *
    59		 * V1 has no Transport binding, so this returns the count vs the
    60		 * empty baseline (same as `opCount()` for a never-flushed session).
    61		 * V2 makes this interesting once Transport surface is bound.
    62		 */
    63		pendingOpCount(): number;
    64	
    65		/** V2 V3 step 3 helper: `true` when local ops haven't been flushed. */
    66		hasPendingFlush(): boolean;
    67	
    68		/** Peer ID of this session, as a BigInt (u64-domain). */
    69		peerId(): bigint;
    70	}
    71	
    72	export interface CollabSessionConstructor {
    73		new(peerId: bigint): CollabSessionInstance;
    74	
    75		/** Reconstruct a session from a previously-exported snapshot. */
    76		fromSnapshot(peerId: bigint, bytes: Uint8Array): CollabSessionInstance;

     6	/**
     7	 * Phase 5.7 V1 (2026-05-22) -- thin TypeScript wrapper over the napi
     8	 * `CollabSession` class.
     9	 *
    10	 * The wrapper exists so:
    11	 * 1. Consumer code imports a STABLE TS-side identifier
    12	 *    (`QuantbookSession`) instead of reaching into a dynamically-loaded
    13	 *    module. The loader is hot-pathed once at startup; the wrapper
    14	 *    holds the resolved native class.
    15	 * 2. We can attach JSDoc + IDE-side validation that the napi layer
    16	 *    doesn't (e.g., reject negative `sheet` integers before the BigInt
    17	 *    boundary makes the error less actionable).
    18	 * 3. V2 / V3 can grow the wrapper without changing the napi surface
    19	 *    (e.g., add async retry wrappers around `mergeBytes`).
    20	 *
    21	 * **Per V1 scope:** no Transport, no presence, no undo, no formula
    22	 * support. See `.plans/_active.md` (engine repo) for V2 deferred list.
    23	 */
    24	
    25	import { loadQuantbookEngine } from './loader';
    26	import type { CollabSessionInstance } from './types';
    27	
    28	/**
    29	 * Construct a fresh `CollabSession` for the given peer.
    30	 *
    31	 * @param peerId Non-zero u64-domain peer identifier. Throws if `0n`,
    32	 *               negative, or doesn't fit in u64.
    33	 */
    34	export function createSession(peerId: bigint): CollabSessionInstance {
    35		const engine = loadQuantbookEngine();
    36		return new engine.CollabSession(peerId);
    37	}
    38	
    39	/**
    40	 * Reconstruct a `CollabSession` from a previously-exported snapshot.
    41	 *
    42	 * @param peerId Non-zero u64-domain peer identifier for THIS session
    43	 *               (need not match the snapshot's origin peer; CRDT will
    44	 *               author new ops under this id).
    45	 * @param bytes  Snapshot bytes from another session's `exportBytes()`.
    46	 */
    47	export function sessionFromSnapshot(peerId: bigint, bytes: Uint8Array): CollabSessionInstance {
    48		const engine = loadQuantbookEngine();
    49		return engine.CollabSession.fromSnapshot(peerId, bytes);
    50	}
    51	
    52	/**
    53	 * Return the engine binding crate's version string. For diagnostics
    54	 * + the V2 version-mismatch error path.
    55	 */
    56	export function quantbookEngineVersion(): string {
    57		return loadQuantbookEngine().version();
    58	}

     6	/**
     7	 * Phase 5.7 V1 (2026-05-22) -- ql-bindings-node loader.
     8	 *
     9	 * Locates and loads the engine's compiled cdylib (`.dylib` on macOS,
    10	 * `.so` on Linux, `.dll` on Windows). Resolves via the environment
    11	 * variable `QUANTBOOK_ENGINE_PATH` first; falls back to the workspace-
    12	 * relative dev path otherwise. **Per CLAUDE.md no-fallback rule**:
    13	 * "fallback" here is a path-resolution alternative, not an error-
    14	 * masking behavior. If both resolutions fail OR the file isn't loadable,
    15	 * we throw LOUDLY with a precise diagnostic.
    16	 *
    17	 * V1 dev-path resolution:
    18	 *   quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/target/release/libql_bindings_node.<ext>
    19	 * computed relative to the IDE workspace root (the parent of
    20	 * `quantlab/` which is the IDE-fork repo).
    21	 *
    22	 * V2 publish pipeline (deferred):
    23	 *   Use `@napi-rs/cli` or a custom artifact pipeline to ship the .node
    24	 *   file as part of the extension package. V1's dev-path approach
    25	 *   requires both engine + IDE repos checked out side-by-side at
    26	 *   ~/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/.
    27	 */
    28	
    29	import * as os from 'os';
    30	import * as path from 'path';
    31	import * as fs from 'fs';
    32	
    33	import type { QuantbookNativeModule } from './types';
    34	
    35	let cachedModule: QuantbookNativeModule | undefined;
    36	
    37	/**
    38	 * Resolve the absolute path to the cdylib for the current platform.
    39	 * Returns the absolute path (regardless of whether the file exists);
    40	 * existence is checked in {@link loadQuantbookEngine}.
    41	 *
    42	 * Resolution order:
    43	 * 1. `QUANTBOOK_ENGINE_PATH` env var, if set + non-empty.
    44	 * 2. V1 dev-path discovery: walk UP from `__dirname` until we find
    45	 *    the IDE repo root (identified by `package.json` with
    46	 *    `name: "code-oss-dev"`), then `..` to the parent workspace dir,
    47	 *    then descend to `quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/target/release`.
    48	 *
    49	 * The IDE-repo-root anchor is independent of whether `__dirname` is
    50	 * the un-compiled `src/quantbook` or the compiled `out/src/quantbook`
    51	 * (differs by one segment, broke the original hard-coded `..` count).
    52	 */
    53	export function resolveEnginePath(): string {
    54		const env = process.env.QUANTBOOK_ENGINE_PATH;
    55		if (typeof env === 'string' && env.length > 0) {
    56			return path.resolve(env);
    57		}
    58		const ext = nativeLibExt();
    59		const ideRepoRoot = findIdeRepoRoot(__dirname);
    60		if (ideRepoRoot === undefined) {
    61			// Fall through to a coarse path with __dirname so the existence
    62			// check in loadQuantbookEngine surfaces a useful diagnostic
    63			// (the error message will include this path).
    64			return path.resolve(
    65				__dirname,
    66				'..', '..', '..', '..', '..', '..',
    67				'quantlab-quantbook', 'quantbook-engine', 'target', 'release',
    68				`libql_bindings_node.${ext}`,
    69			);
    70		}
    71		return path.resolve(
    72			ideRepoRoot,
    73			'..', 'quantlab-quantbook', 'quantbook-engine', 'target', 'release',
    74			`libql_bindings_node.${ext}`,
    75		);
    76	}
    77	
    78	/**
    79	 * Walk UP from `start` looking for the IDE repo's `package.json`
    80	 * (identified by `name: "code-oss-dev"`). Returns the absolute path
    81	 * to the directory containing that package.json, or `undefined` if
    82	 * not found within the filesystem-root traversal limit.
    83	 */
    84	function findIdeRepoRoot(start: string): string | undefined {
    85		let cur = path.resolve(start);
    86		for (let depth = 0; depth < 16; depth += 1) {
    87			const pkgPath = path.join(cur, 'package.json');
    88			if (fs.existsSync(pkgPath)) {
    89				try {
    90					const raw = fs.readFileSync(pkgPath, 'utf8');
    91					const pkg = JSON.parse(raw) as { name?: string };
    92					if (pkg.name === 'code-oss-dev') {
    93						return cur;
    94					}
    95				} catch {
    96					// Malformed package.json or read failure -- keep walking.
    97					// We're scanning ancestors; don't surface a partial read.
    98				}
    99			}
   100			const parent = path.dirname(cur);
   101			if (parent === cur) {
   102				return undefined;
   103			}
   104			cur = parent;
   105		}
   106		return undefined;
   107	}
   108	
   109	function nativeLibExt(): string {
   110		switch (process.platform) {
   111			case 'darwin': return 'dylib';
   112			case 'linux': return 'so';
   113			case 'win32': return 'dll';
   114			default:
   115				throw new Error(
   116					`Unsupported platform "${process.platform}" for ql-bindings-node V1. ` +
   117					`Supported: darwin, linux, win32.`,
   118				);
   119		}
   120	}
   121	
   122	/**
   123	 * Load and return the engine native module. Cached after first load.
   124	 *
   125	 * **Failure modes (all surface LOUDLY)**:
   126	 * - `QUANTBOOK_ENGINE_PATH` set but file doesn't exist
   127	 * - Default dev-path doesn't exist (engine not built, or wrong
   128	 *   workspace layout)
   129	 * - File exists but `require()` throws (binary built for wrong
   130	 *   platform, wrong Node ABI, missing symbols, etc.)
   131	 *
   132	 * Each failure mode emits a distinct diagnostic so the user knows
   133	 * what to fix.
   134	 *
   135	 * **Why .dylib / .so / .dll not .node**: V1 loads the raw cdylib
   136	 * directly. Node.js's `process.dlopen` accepts any extension at the
   137	 * native level; the `.node` extension is just a convention from
   138	 * `@napi-rs/cli`. V2 publish pipeline will produce `.node` files.
   139	 */
   140	export function loadQuantbookEngine(): QuantbookNativeModule {
   141		if (cachedModule !== undefined) {
   142			return cachedModule;
   143		}
   144		const enginePath = resolveEnginePath();
   145		if (!fs.existsSync(enginePath)) {
   146			throw new Error(
   147				`Quantbook engine binary not found at ${enginePath}. ` +
   148				`Either set QUANTBOOK_ENGINE_PATH or build the engine with: ` +
   149				`cd ../quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine && cargo build -p ql-bindings-node --release`,
   150			);
   151		}
   152		// process.dlopen does the actual loading. Build a fake CommonJS
   153		// module first so dlopen has somewhere to attach exports.
   154		// `as unknown as NodeJS.Module` is the documented Node.js pattern
   155		// for non-.node files (the `unknown` step makes the cast explicit
   156		// rather than dangerous). V2 publish pipeline will switch to
   157		// `require()` + `.node` extension which eliminates the cast.
   158		const fakeModule: { exports: Record<string, unknown> } = { exports: {} };
   159		try {
   160			process.dlopen(fakeModule as unknown as NodeJS.Module, enginePath);
   161		} catch (err) {
   162			const detail = err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err);
   163			throw new Error(
   164				`Failed to dlopen Quantbook engine at ${enginePath}: ${detail}. ` +
   165				`Check that the binary matches your platform (got platform=${process.platform}, arch=${process.arch}) ` +
   166				`and Node ABI (got Node ${process.version}).`,
   167			);
   168		}
   169		cachedModule = fakeModule.exports as unknown as QuantbookNativeModule;
   170		// Smoke-check the expected surface is present. Don't silently
   171		// accept a load that returned an empty / partial export set.
   172		if (typeof cachedModule.version !== 'function' || typeof cachedModule.CollabSession !== 'function') {
   173			throw new Error(
   174				`Quantbook engine at ${enginePath} loaded but is missing expected exports. ` +
   175				`Got: ${JSON.stringify(Object.keys(cachedModule))}. ` +
   176				`Expected at least: version(), CollabSession constructor.`,
   177			);
   178		}
   179		return cachedModule;
   180	}
   181	
   182	/**
   183	 * Test helper -- reset the module cache so unit tests can re-load.
   184	 * Production code should not call this.
   185	 *
   186	 * @internal
   187	 */
   188	export function _resetQuantbookEngineCacheForTests(): void {
   189		cachedModule = undefined;
   190	}
   191	
   192	/**
   193	 * Return host info string for diagnostics. Not load-bearing; used in
   194	 * status displays + error messages.
   195	 */
   196	export function quantbookHostInfo(): string {
   197		return [
   198			`node=${process.version}`,
   199			`platform=${process.platform}`,
   200			`arch=${process.arch}`,
   201			`uptime=${process.uptime().toFixed(1)}s`,
   202			`tmpdir=${os.tmpdir()}`,
   203		].join(' ');
   204	}

     5	
     6	/**
     7	 * Phase 5.7 V1 (2026-05-22) -- Quantbook commands.
     8	 *
     9	 * Registers the "Quantbook: Demo Round-Trip" command, which drives a
    10	 * peer A -> bytes -> peer B round-trip via the engine binding. Uses
    11	 * VS Code's native InformationMessage + Output Channel for UI rather
    12	 * than a webview -- this is a smoke demo proving the engine works in
    13	 * the extension host, NOT a real cell-grid UI.
    14	 *
    15	 * Per the V1 scope (`.plans/_active.md`):
    16	 *   - One Op variant exposed (PutValue)
    17	 *   - No Transport binding; sync is via exportBytes/mergeBytes
    18	 *   - No persistence; sessions are per-command-invocation
    19	 *
    20	 * V2/V3 builds on this: real cell grid, Transport binding, persistence.
    21	 */
    22	
    23	import * as vscode from 'vscode';
    24	
    25	import { createSession, quantbookEngineVersion, sessionFromSnapshot } from '../quantbook/session';
    26	import { quantbookHostInfo } from '../quantbook/loader';
    27	import type { CollabSessionInstance } from '../quantbook/types';
    28	
    29	let outputChannel: vscode.OutputChannel | undefined;
    30	
    31	function getOutput(): vscode.OutputChannel {
    32		if (outputChannel === undefined) {
    33			outputChannel = vscode.window.createOutputChannel('Quantbook');
    34		}
    35		return outputChannel;
    36	}
    37	
    38	function describe(label: string, session: CollabSessionInstance): string {
    39		return `${label}: peerId=${session.peerId()}, opCount=${session.opCount()}, pending=${session.pendingOpCount()}, hasPendingFlush=${session.hasPendingFlush()}`;
    40	}
    41	
    42	/**
    43	 * Demo state held across the run. Two peers; peer B is created on
    44	 * first "Sync to Peer B" action and reused thereafter.
    45	 */
    46	interface DemoState {
    47		peerA: CollabSessionInstance;
    48		peerB: CollabSessionInstance | undefined;
    49	}
    50	
    51	async function runDemoLoop(state: DemoState, log: vscode.OutputChannel): Promise<void> {
    52		while (true) {
    53			const status = describe('peer A', state.peerA) +
    54				(state.peerB !== undefined ? `\n${describe('peer B', state.peerB)}` : '\n(peer B not yet created)');
    55	
    56			const choice = await vscode.window.showInformationMessage(
    57				`Quantbook demo round-trip\n\n${status}`,
    58				{ modal: true },
    59				'Add Random PutValue (peer A)',
    60				'Sync A -> B',
    61				'Sync B -> A',
    62				'Append on peer B',
    63				'Close',
    64			);
    65	
    66			if (choice === undefined || choice === 'Close') {
    67				log.appendLine('Demo closed.');
    68				return;
    69			}
    70	
    71			try {
    72				if (choice === 'Add Random PutValue (peer A)') {
    73					const sheet = 0;
    74					const row = Math.floor(Math.random() * 100);
    75					const col = Math.floor(Math.random() * 10);
    76					const value = Math.round(Math.random() * 10000) / 100;
    77					state.peerA.appendPutValue(sheet, row, col, value);
    78					log.appendLine(`peer A appendPutValue(${sheet}, ${row}, ${col}, ${value}) -> opCount=${state.peerA.opCount()}`);
    79				} else if (choice === 'Sync A -> B') {
    80					const bytes = state.peerA.exportBytes();
    81					log.appendLine(`peer A exportBytes() -> ${bytes.length} bytes`);
    82					if (state.peerB === undefined) {
    83						state.peerB = sessionFromSnapshot(2n, bytes);
    84						log.appendLine(`peer B created via fromSnapshot(2n, ${bytes.length} bytes) -> opCount=${state.peerB.opCount()}`);
    85					} else {
    86						const merged = state.peerB.mergeBytes(bytes);
    87						log.appendLine(`peer B mergeBytes(${bytes.length} bytes) -> merged=${merged}, opCount=${state.peerB.opCount()}`);
    88					}
    89				} else if (choice === 'Sync B -> A') {
    90					if (state.peerB === undefined) {
    91						vscode.window.showWarningMessage('Peer B does not exist yet. Run "Sync A -> B" first.');
    92						continue;
    93					}
    94					const bytes = state.peerB.exportBytes();
    95					log.appendLine(`peer B exportBytes() -> ${bytes.length} bytes`);
    96					const merged = state.peerA.mergeBytes(bytes);
    97					log.appendLine(`peer A mergeBytes(${bytes.length} bytes) -> merged=${merged}, opCount=${state.peerA.opCount()}`);
    98				} else if (choice === 'Append on peer B') {
    99					if (state.peerB === undefined) {
   100						vscode.window.showWarningMessage('Peer B does not exist yet. Run "Sync A -> B" first.');
   101						continue;
   102					}
   103					const sheet = 0;
   104					const row = Math.floor(Math.random() * 100);
   105					const col = Math.floor(Math.random() * 10);
   106					const value = Math.round(Math.random() * 10000) / 100;
   107					state.peerB.appendPutValue(sheet, row, col, value);
   108					log.appendLine(`peer B appendPutValue(${sheet}, ${row}, ${col}, ${value}) -> opCount=${state.peerB.opCount()}`);
   109				}
   110			} catch (err) {
   111				const message = err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err);
   112				log.appendLine(`ERROR: ${message}`);
   113				vscode.window.showErrorMessage(`Quantbook demo error: ${message}`);
   114			}
   115		}
   116	}
   117	
   118	export function registerQuantbookCommands(context: vscode.ExtensionContext): void {
   119		context.subscriptions.push(
   120			vscode.commands.registerCommand('quantlab.quantbookDemo', async () => {
   121				const log = getOutput();
   122				log.show(true);
   123				log.appendLine('=== Quantbook Demo Round-Trip ===');
   124				log.appendLine(`Host: ${quantbookHostInfo()}`);
   125				try {
   126					const version = quantbookEngineVersion();
   127					log.appendLine(`Engine binding version: ${version}`);
   128				} catch (err) {
   129					const detail = err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err);
   130					log.appendLine(`FATAL: failed to load engine binding -- ${detail}`);
   131					vscode.window.showErrorMessage(`Quantbook engine binding failed to load: ${detail}`);
   132					return;
   133				}
   134				let peerA: CollabSessionInstance;
   135				try {
   136					peerA = createSession(1n);
   137					log.appendLine(`peer A created: peerId=${peerA.peerId()}`);
   138				} catch (err) {
   139					const detail = err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err);
   140					log.appendLine(`FATAL: failed to create peer A -- ${detail}`);
   141					vscode.window.showErrorMessage(`Quantbook demo: failed to create session: ${detail}`);
   142					return;
   143				}
   144				await runDemoLoop({ peerA, peerB: undefined }, log);
   145			}),
   146		);
   147	}

     5	
     6	/**
     7	 * Phase 5.7 V1 (2026-05-22) -- engine round-trip test.
     8	 *
     9	 * Loads the `ql-bindings-node` cdylib and exercises the minimum round-
    10	 * trip: peer A appends a PutValue -> exports bytes -> peer B merges
    11	 * bytes -> peer B's op count matches.
    12	 *
    13	 * **Prerequisite**: engine binding must be built. Skips loudly (with
    14	 * a once-per-process stderr log) if the binary is missing. Mirrors
    15	 * the existing `qviz-daemon-client.test.ts` skip-on-missing-prereq
    16	 * pattern; the binary isn't required for THIS extension's tests in
    17	 * isolation (CI may run without the engine repo checked out).
    18	 *
    19	 * Build the binary before running:
    20	 *   cd ../quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine
    21	 *   cargo build -p ql-bindings-node --release
    22	 */
    23	
    24	import * as assert from 'assert';
    25	import * as fs from 'fs';
    26	
    27	import {
    28		loadQuantbookEngine,
    29		resolveEnginePath,
    30		_resetQuantbookEngineCacheForTests,
    31	} from '../src/quantbook/loader';
    32	import {
    33		createSession,
    34		quantbookEngineVersion,
    35		sessionFromSnapshot,
    36	} from '../src/quantbook/session';
    37	
    38	function engineAvailable(): { ok: true } | { ok: false; reason: string } {
    39		const enginePath = resolveEnginePath();
    40		if (!fs.existsSync(enginePath)) {
    41			return {
    42				ok: false,
    43				reason: `engine binary not found at ${enginePath} -- build with: cd ../quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine && cargo build -p ql-bindings-node --release`,
    44			};
    45		}
    46		return { ok: true };
    47	}
    48	
    49	function shouldSkip(): { skip: boolean; reason?: string } {
    50		const r = engineAvailable();
    51		if (!r.ok) {
    52			const seen = global as unknown as { __quantbookEngineSkipLogged?: boolean };
    53			if (!seen.__quantbookEngineSkipLogged) {
    54				seen.__quantbookEngineSkipLogged = true;
    55				console.warn(`[quantbook-roundtrip.test] SKIPPING: ${r.reason}`);
    56			}
    57			return { skip: true, reason: r.reason };
    58		}
    59		return { skip: false };
    60	}
    61	
    62	suite('quantbook engine round-trip -- Phase 5.7 V1', () => {
    63	
    64		suiteSetup(function () {
    65			const { skip } = shouldSkip();
    66			if (skip) {
    67				this.skip();
    68			}
    69			_resetQuantbookEngineCacheForTests();
    70			// **V1 (2026-05-22):** dlopen of the 3.5MB cdylib can take
    71			// 10-30s on first load (cold disk + symbol resolution + Loro
    72			// crate initialization). Without preloading, the first test
    73			// to call any engine method hits mocha's default 10s timeout.
    74			// Preload here so the dlopen cost is amortized in suiteSetup,
    75			// not the first test body. Bumping suiteSetup timeout to 60s
    76			// to be safe.
    77			this.timeout(60000);
    78			loadQuantbookEngine();
    79		});
    80	
    81		test('version() returns a non-empty semver-ish string', () => {
    82			const v = quantbookEngineVersion();
    83			assert.ok(typeof v === 'string', 'version is a string');
    84			assert.ok(v.length > 0, 'version is non-empty');
    85			assert.ok(/\d+\.\d+\.\d+/.test(v), `version looks like x.y.z, got: ${v}`);
    86		});
    87	
    88		test('CollabSession constructor accepts BigInt peer ID', () => {
    89			const s = createSession(1n);
    90			assert.strictEqual(s.peerId(), 1n);
    91			assert.strictEqual(s.opCount(), 0);
    92			assert.strictEqual(s.pendingOpCount(), 0);
    93			assert.strictEqual(s.hasPendingFlush(), false);
    94		});
    95	
    96		test('CollabSession constructor rejects 0n (PeerId sentinel)', () => {
    97			assert.throws(() => createSession(0n), /peerId/i);
    98		});
    99	
   100		test('CollabSession constructor rejects negative BigInt', () => {
   101			assert.throws(() => createSession(-1n), /non-negative|negative/i);
   102		});
   103	
   104		test('CollabSession constructor rejects BigInt > u64', () => {
   105			// 2^64 = 18446744073709551616 (one past u64::MAX which is 2^64 - 1)
   106			const tooBig = 1n << 64n;
   107			assert.throws(() => createSession(tooBig), /lossy|fit/i);
   108		});
   109	
   110		test('appendPutValue increments opCount + sets hasPendingFlush', () => {
   111			const s = createSession(1n);
   112			s.appendPutValue(0, 0, 0, 42);
   113			assert.strictEqual(s.opCount(), 1);
   114			assert.strictEqual(s.pendingOpCount(), 1);
   115			assert.strictEqual(s.hasPendingFlush(), true);
   116		});
   117	
   118		test('round-trip: peer A append -> export -> peer B merge -> peer B op count matches', () => {
   119			const peerA = createSession(1n);
   120			peerA.appendPutValue(0, 0, 0, 42);
   121			peerA.appendPutValue(0, 0, 1, 100);

    30	const PYTHON_PATH =

    39	// worked when tests ran from `test/` itself.
    40	const PYTHON_DIR = path.resolve(__dirname, '..', '..', 'python');

    43	function pythonAvailable(): { ok: true } | { ok: false; reason: string } {
    44		try {
    45			fs.accessSync(PYTHON_PATH, fs.constants.X_OK);
    46		} catch {
    47			return { ok: false, reason: `python interpreter not executable at ${PYTHON_PATH} (set QUANTLAB_TEST_PYTHON to override)` };
    48		}
    49		try {
    50			fs.accessSync(SPIKE_DATA, fs.constants.R_OK);
    51		} catch {

    53		}
    54		return { ok: true };

    57	// Backwards-compat boolean used in `if (skip) this.skip()` patterns below.
    58	function shouldSkip(): boolean {
    59		const r = pythonAvailable();

    62			const seen = (global as { __qvizDaemonSkipLogged?: boolean });

    71	}

    73	function makeWorkspace(): string {

    75		const dataDir = path.join(root, 'data');

   268	impl NapiFn {
   269	  fn gen_arg_conversions(&self) -> BindgenResult<ArgConversions> {
   270	    let mut arg_conversions = vec![];
   271	    let mut args = vec![];
   272	    let mut refs = vec![];
   273	    let mut mut_ref_spans = vec![];

   275	    // fetch this
   276	    if let Some(parent) = &self.parent {
   277	      match self.fn_self {
   278	        Some(FnSelf::Ref) => {
   279	          refs.push(make_ref(quote! { cb.this }));

   281	            let this_ptr = cb.unwrap_raw::<#parent>()?;
   282	            let this: &#parent = Box::leak(Box::from_raw(this_ptr));
   283	          });
   284	        }
   285	        Some(FnSelf::MutRef) => {
   286	          refs.push(make_ref(quote! { cb.this }));
   287	          arg_conversions.push(quote! {
   288	            let this_ptr = cb.unwrap_raw::<#parent>()?;
   289	            let this: &mut #parent = Box::leak(Box::from_raw(this_ptr));
   290	          });
   291	        }

   293	      };
   294	    }

   296	    let mut skipped_arg_count = 0;

   299	      let ident = Ident::new(&format!("arg{i}"), Span::call_site());
   300	
   301	      match &arg.kind {
   302	        NapiFnArgKind::PatType(path) => {

   304	            args.push(quote! { __wrapped_env });
   305	            skipped_arg_count += 1;
   306	          } else {
   307	            let is_in_class = self.parent.is_some();

   310	              // get `Reference` in `napi::bindgen_prelude::Reference`

   312	                if p.ident == "Reference" {
   313	                  if !is_in_class {
   314	                    bail_span!(p, "`Reference` is only allowed in class methods");
   315	                  }
   316	                  // get `FooStruct` in `Reference<FooStruct>`
   317	                  if let syn::PathArguments::AngleBracketed(syn::AngleBracketedGenericArguments {
   318	                    args: angle_bracketed_args,
   319	                    ..
   320	                  }) = &p.arguments

   322	                    if let Some(syn::GenericArgument::Type(syn::Type::Path(path))) =

   324	                    {

   333	                      }
   334	                    }
   335	                  }
   336	                } else if p.ident == "This" {

   341	                  }) = &p.arguments

    27	[workspace.package]
    28	version = "0.1.0"
    29	edition = "2021"
    30	# MSRV floor 1.85: required for edition2024 (clap_lex 1.1.0 transitively via criterion). Day 2
    31	# bump from 1.83. Build is pinned to 1.95.0 in rust-toolchain.toml.
    32	rust-version = "1.85"
    33	license = "MIT OR Apache-2.0"
    34	publish = false
    35	
    36	[workspace.dependencies]
    37	# Apache Arrow — exact pins for Phase 0. All 7 crates kept in lock-step at the same patch
    38	# (58.3.0 is the latest 58.x patch as of 2026-05-11; arrow-array 58.1.0's `^58` transitive
    39	# would otherwise drift arrow-buffer to 58.3 and break the pin guard).
    40	arrow = "=58.3.0"
    41	arrow-array = "=58.3.0"
    42	arrow-buffer = "=58.3.0"
    43	arrow-schema = "=58.3.0"
    44	arrow-select = "=58.3.0"
    45	arrow-arith = "=58.3.0"
    46	arrow-cast = "=58.3.0"
    47	
    48	# Concurrency
    49	rayon = "=1.12.0"
    50	tokio = { version = "=1.50.0", default-features = false, features = ["rt-multi-thread", "macros", "fs", "net", "time", "sync"] }
    51	
    52	# Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 4 (2026-05-21) — WebSocket transport in ql-collab-ws.
    53	# tokio-tungstenite is the de-facto async websocket client for tokio.
    54	# futures-util provides SplitSink / SplitStream + StreamExt::next /
    55	# SinkExt::send. MVP scope: no TLS feature flags (plain ws:// only);
    56	# V2 V4 adds rustls. tokio-tungstenite 0.29.0 depends on tokio ^1.0
    57	# (compat with our =1.50.0 pin) and futures-util ^0.3.28.
    58	tokio-tungstenite = "=0.29.0"
    59	futures-util = { version = "=0.3.32", default-features = false, features = ["std", "sink"] }
    60	
    61	# Phase 5.5 V2 V4 V1 step 3 (2026-05-21) — Tier J3 + K5. Compile-time
    62	# `assert_impl_all!` proofs for `WebSocketTransport: Sync` and
    63	# `WebSocketError: Send + Sync`. (The V2 V3 step 4 docstring claimed
    64	# !Sync; step 3 implementation revealed the type is actually Sync —
    65	# all fields happen to be Sync; the single-consumer mpsc constraint
    66	# is enforced by `try_recv(&mut self)`, not by !Sync.)
    67	# `static_assertions` is a tiny no-runtime-cost crate exposing the
    68	# `assert_impl_all!` macro. Used only by ql-collab-ws.
    69	static_assertions = "=1.1.0"
    70	
    71	# Phase 5.7 V1 (2026-05-22) — VS Code extension binding via napi-rs.
    72	# `ql-bindings-node` builds a `.node` cdylib loaded by the quantlab
    73	# VS Code fork's extension host (Node.js). napi-rs 3.x is the current
    74	# major; napi-derive provides the `#[napi]` proc-macro; napi-build is
    75	# the build.rs helper that locates Node headers + emits cargo:rustc-cfg
    76	# directives. cdylib output naming differs per platform (.dylib / .so /
    77	# .dll); the @napi-rs/cli npm tool typically renames to `.node`, but
    78	# for V1 we load the raw cdylib via an explicit path in TS code.
    79	napi = { version = "=3.9.0", default-features = false, features = ["napi8"] }
    80	napi-derive = "=3.5.6"
    81	napi-build = "=2.3.2"
    82	
    83	# Bench
    84	criterion = { version = "=0.8.2", default-features = false, features = ["html_reports"] }
    85	# Property-based testing for invariant checks (ql-storage, ql-calcgraph, ql-exec dev-deps)
    86	proptest = "=1.8.0"
    87	
    88	# SIMD (runtime dispatch per Phase 0 Decision F).
    89	# pulp's default features include `x86-v3` (= AVX2 baseline at compile time), which would
    90	# defeat A3 by baking AVX2 into distributable binaries. We disable defaults and opt back in
    91	# to `std` only; runtime dispatch comes from pulp's per-function `Arch::detect()` and
    92	# `multiversion`. Do NOT add `x86-v3`/`x86-v4` to features here.
    93	pulp = { version = "=0.22.2", default-features = false, features = ["std"] }
    94	multiversion = "=0.8.0"
    95	bytemuck = "=1.25.0"
    96	
    97	# Python interop (Phase 5; reserved in workspace pins for consistency)
    98	pyo3 = { version = "=0.28.3", default-features = false, features = ["extension-module", "abi3-py310"] }
    99	
   100	# WASM (later phases). Pinned to 0.2.121 (latest 2026-05-11; the older 0.2.118 drifted via
   101	# transitive resolution after toolchain bump 1.83→1.95 — see scripts/check-cargo-lock-pins.sh).
   102	wasm-bindgen = "=0.2.121"
   103	
   104	# Storage / formats (later phases)
   105	polars = { version = "=0.53.0", default-features = false, features = ["lazy", "fmt", "parquet"] }
   106	datafusion = { version = "=53.1.0", default-features = false }
   107	duckdb = { version = "=1.10502.0", default-features = false, features = ["bundled", "appender-arrow"] }
   108	calamine = "=0.34.0"
   109	
   110	# Reactive (salsa = experiments only per Decision A) / collab
   111	salsa = "=0.26.2"
   112	loro = "=1.12.0"
   113	
   114	# Utility
   115	thiserror = "=2.0.18"
   116	tracing = "=0.1.44"
   117	dashmap = "=6.1.0"
   118	petgraph = { version = "=0.8.3", default-features = false, features = ["std"] }
   119	tempfile = "=3.27.0"
   120	mimalloc = { version = "=0.1.48", default-features = false }
   121	num_cpus = "=1.17.0"
   122	# Phase 2A.10 audit H3/M14: SipHash24 with a fixed seed for stable formula
   123	# fingerprints. `siphasher` pins the algorithm contract (unlike stdlib
   124	# `DefaultHasher` whose internal algorithm is not specified). 800 MB/s
   125	# throughput is plenty for fingerprint use; we don't need BLAKE3's
   126	# cryptographic strength.
   127	siphasher = "=1.0.1"
   128	
   129	# Serialization (graph-profile.json export — OG-06; `.qbook/workbook.toml` envelope per

   131	serde = { version = "=1.0.228", features = ["derive"] }

   133	toml = "=0.9.7"
   134	
   135	# Statistical distributions (Wave 3 distributions batch: NORM.* / T.* /

   137	# the same crate at the same pin — matching their version for canon
   138	# behavioral compatibility. `default-features = false` keeps the crate
   139	# minimal (no rand integration).
   140	statrs = { version = "=0.18.0", default-features = false }
   141

codex
The napi-rs macro source is important here: normal `#[napi]` methods are only wrapped in `catch_unwind` when the attribute explicitly sets `catch_unwind`; this crate does not. Also, generated `&mut self` methods recover a raw wrapped pointer and materialize `&mut T`, with no Rust-side mutex in the generated wrapper.

const { _resetQuantbookEngineCacheForTests } = require('./extensions/quantlab/out/src/quantbook/loader.js');
const { createSession } = require('./extensions/quantlab/out/src/quantbook/session.js');
const cases = [
  ['0n', 0n, false],
  ['1n', 1n, true],
  ['2"'^64-1'"', (1n << 64n) - 1n, null],
  ['2"'^64'"', 1n << 64n, false],
  ['2"'^65'"', 1n << 65n, false],
  ['i64::MAX', 9223372036854775807n, true],
  ['i64::MAX+1', 9223372036854775808n, true],
  ['-1n', -1n, false],
  ['-(2"'^64-1)'"', -((1n << 64n) - 1n), false],
  ['Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER', Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER, false],
  ['123 Number', 123, false],
];
for (const [label, value, shouldPass] of cases) {
  _resetQuantbookEngineCacheForTests();
  try {
    const s = createSession(value);
    console.log(label, 'PASS', String(s.peerId()));
  } catch (e) {
    console.log(label, 'ERR', e && e.message ? e.message : String(e));
  }
}

     1	//! Per-peer identifier for Phase 5 multi-user collaboration.
     2	//!
     3	//! **History:**
     4	//! - **Phase 5.2.a (2026-05-19):** introduced as `ql_collab::PeerId`.
     5	//! - **Phase 5.2 D-1 step 1 (2026-05-19):** moved to `ql_oplog::PeerId`
     6	//!   so the op-log layer (where ops are tagged with peer-of-origin)
     7	//!   could reference it without a reverse `ql-oplog → ql-collab` dep.
     8	//! - **Phase 5.2 D-1 step 1.1 (2026-05-19):** moved AGAIN to
     9	//!   `ql_types::PeerId` after the step-1 audit (Codex MEDIUM-3) caught
    10	//!   a forthcoming Cargo cycle: `ql-storage::FormatId::Custom(PeerId, _)`
    11	//!   (step 3) cannot reference a type living in `ql-oplog`, because
    12	//!   `ql-oplog` already depends on `ql-storage`. `ql-types` is the true
    13	//!   dependency floor — both `ql-storage` and `ql-oplog` depend on it.
    14	//!   `ql-oplog` and `ql-collab` re-export `ql_types::PeerId` for
    15	//!   back-compat; external callers using `ql_oplog::PeerId` or
    16	//!   `ql_collab::PeerId` keep working.
    17	//!
    18	//! `PeerId` is a thin newtype around `u64` matching Loro's native peer-id
    19	//! type. Stable per-session; the user-facing collaboration tooling
    20	//! decides how to assign IDs (random vs user-derived vs server-allocated).
    21	//!
    22	//! ## Wire format
    23	//!
    24	//! `serde` derives are `transparent`, so a `PeerId` serializes as a
    25	//! plain `u64` in JSON / CBOR / etc. — matches the on-the-wire
    26	//! convention for Loro peer ids and avoids a wrapped form like `[42]`.
    27	//!
    28	//! ## Consumers
    29	//!
    30	//! - `ql_storage::FormatId::Custom(PeerId, u32)` (Phase 5.2 D-1 step 3).
    31	//! - `ql_oplog::wire::FormatIdWire` + `Op::RegisterFormat/SetCellFormat`
    32	//!   (Phase 5.2 D-1 step 2 + step 4).
    33	//! - `ql_oplog::OpLog::set_peer_id` — wires through to Loro's
    34	//!   `LoroDoc::set_peer_id` so concurrent appends carry the right
    35	//!   origin in the CRDT merge metadata (Phase 5.2.b).
    36	//! - `ql_collab::CollabSession` + `ql_collab::presence` (Phase 5.2.a
    37	//!   onward).
    38	
    39	use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
    40	
    41	/// Per-peer collaboration identifier.
    42	///
    43	/// Wraps a `u64` to match Loro's native peer-id type. Stable for
    44	/// the lifetime of a `CollabSession`; the user-facing tooling
    45	/// decides assignment policy. Two concurrent peers MUST have
    46	/// distinct ids — duplicate peer ids corrupt the document via
    47	/// conflicting OpIDs (Loro pitfall, surfaced by Phase 5.1 audit
    48	/// then closed by Phase 5.2.b wiring). Prefer per-process-random
    49	/// allocation unless your server enforces uniqueness explicitly.
    50	///
    51	/// Serde shape is `#[serde(transparent)]` — wire format is a plain
    52	/// `u64`, not a wrapped tuple.
    53	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, Hash, PartialEq, Ord, PartialOrd, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    54	#[serde(transparent)]
    55	pub struct PeerId(pub u64);
    56	
    57	impl PeerId {
    58	    /// Construct a `PeerId` from the raw u64.
    59	    pub const fn new(id: u64) -> Self {
    60	        Self(id)
    61	    }
    62	
    63	    /// Unwrap to the raw `u64`. Used at the Loro boundary
    64	    /// (`LoroDoc::set_peer_id` takes `u64`).
    65	    pub const fn as_u64(self) -> u64 {
    66	        self.0
    67	    }
    68	}
    69	
    70	impl From<u64> for PeerId {
    71	    fn from(id: u64) -> Self {
    72	        Self(id)
    73	    }
    74	}
    75	
    76	impl From<PeerId> for u64 {
    77	    fn from(p: PeerId) -> Self {
    78	        p.0
    79	    }
    80	}
    81	
    82	impl std::fmt::Display for PeerId {
    83	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
    84	        // 16-hex-char form keeps display compact and easy to spot in logs.
    85	        write!(f, "{:016x}", self.0)
    86	    }
    87	}
    88	
    89	/// **Phase 5.2 D-1 step 1.1 (2026-05-19):** sentinel peer-id used by
    90	/// the `.qbook` envelope's legacy-u32 → tagged-tuple `FormatId`
    91	/// migration (Phase 5.2 D-1 step 5).
    92	///
    93	/// Old `.qbook` files encoded `FormatId` as a bare `u32`. On load,
    94	/// values `≤ 163` map to `FormatId::Builtin(n)`; values `> 163` map
    95	/// to `FormatId::Custom(LEGACY_PEER, n - 164)`. After the migration
    96	/// runs, the workbook resaves with the new tagged-tuple schema and
    97	/// `LEGACY_PEER` only appears in custom format ids inherited from
    98	/// pre-Phase-5.2 saves.
    99	///
   100	/// **Choice of `0`:** PeerId(0) is accepted by Loro as a valid
   101	/// peer-id but is documented (Phase 5.2.b) as something production
   102	/// callers MUST avoid (Loro requires distinct concurrent peer ids).
   103	/// LegacyPeer claiming `0` is therefore safe — no live peer should
   104	/// collide. Alternative `u64::MAX - 1` was considered but `0` is the
   105	/// more obvious sentinel (matches Unix `uid=0=root` convention).
   106	///
   107	/// Cross-ref: `docs/phase5/d-1-starting-checklist.md` § "Step 5".
   108	pub const LEGACY_PEER: PeerId = PeerId::new(0);
   109

   120	    }
   121

   125	        assert_eq!(format!("{p}"), "0000000000001234");
   126	    }

  1737	/// **Phase 5.4 V2 V1.1 (2026-05-19):** RAII guard returned by
  1738	/// [`CollabSession::start_undo_group_scoped`]. `Drop` calls
  1739	/// `end_undo_group` automatically — runs on scope exit, on `?`
  1740	/// propagation, AND on panic-unwind. Use this in any code path
  1741	/// that can fail mid-group; the manual `start_undo_group` /
  1742	/// `end_undo_group` pair only handles the happy path.
  1743	///
  1744	/// Implements `Deref<Target = CollabSession>` + `DerefMut`, so
  1745	/// all `CollabSession` methods are callable directly on the
  1746	/// guard during its lifetime.
  1747	pub struct UndoGroupGuard<'a> {
  1748	    session: &'a mut CollabSession,
  1749	}
  1750	
  1751	impl std::fmt::Debug for UndoGroupGuard<'_> {
  1752	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
  1753	        f.debug_struct("UndoGroupGuard")
  1754	            .field("session", &self.session)
  1755	            .finish()
  1756	    }
  1757	}
  1758	
  1759	impl std::ops::Deref for UndoGroupGuard<'_> {
  1760	    type Target = CollabSession;
  1761	    fn deref(&self) -> &CollabSession {
  1762	        self.session
  1763	    }
  1764	}
  1765	
  1766	impl std::ops::DerefMut for UndoGroupGuard<'_> {
  1767	    fn deref_mut(&mut self) -> &mut CollabSession {
  1768	        self.session
  1769	    }
  1770	}
  1771	
  1772	impl Drop for UndoGroupGuard<'_> {
  1773	    fn drop(&mut self) {
  1774	        // `end_undo_group` is infallible per Loro 1.12.0; safe in
  1775	        // Drop. If the group has already been closed manually
  1776	        // (shouldn't happen via this guard, but defensively), Loro
  1777	        // treats it as a no-op.
  1778	        self.session.end_undo_group();
  1779	    }
  1780	}
  1781	
  1782	/// Construct + configure a Loro `UndoManager` bound to `log`'s
  1783	/// underlying doc.
  1784	///
  1785	/// Phase 5.4 V1 setup: register `PRESENCE_COMMIT_ORIGIN` as an
  1786	/// exclude prefix so cursor-movement commits don't fill the
  1787	/// undo stack. (Phase 5.6 V1 tags presence writes with that
  1788	/// origin via `OpLog::presence_set` / `presence_remove`.)
  1789	fn make_undo_manager(log: &OpLog) -> loro::UndoManager {
  1790	    let mut undo = log.new_undo_manager();
  1791	    undo.add_exclude_origin_prefix(PRESENCE_COMMIT_ORIGIN);
  1792	    undo
  1793	}

     1	//! The `Op` enum — the engine's mutation vocabulary for the op log.
     2	//!
     3	//! Phase 2A.3.a (2026-05-12): minimum producer set covering everything
     4	//! `WorkbookRuntime` and `WorkbookTransaction` can mutate through their
     5	//! public API. Future variants (sheet renames, sheet deletes, formatting
     6	//! changes) land alongside the API additions that produce them.
     7	//!
     8	//! Each variant mirrors a `Workbook` mutation method one-to-one:
     9	//!
    10	//! | `Op` variant       | Replays to                                       |
    11	//! |--------------------|--------------------------------------------------|
    12	//! | `PutValue`         | `Workbook::put_at`                               |
    13	//! | `PutFormula`       | `Workbook::put_formula`                          |
    14	//! | `ClearFormula`     | `Workbook::clear_formula`                        |
    15	//! | `SetName`          | `Workbook::set_name`                             |
    16	//! | `AddSheet`         | `Workbook::add_sheet_with_chunk_rows`            |
    17	//! | `RegisterFormat`   | `FormatTable::register_at` (W5-80)               |
    18	//! | `SetCellFormat`    | `CellFormatOverlay::set` / `::clear` (W5-80)     |
    19	//! | `BatchCommit`      | (recursive — applies each inner op in order)     |
    20	//!
    21	//! ## Wire format choices
    22	//!
    23	//! - `Arc<str>` is NOT used in wire fields. `serde` without the `rc`
    24	//!   feature can't deserialize `Arc<str>` directly. We store `String` on
    25	//!   the wire and convert at the replay boundary. Cleaner separation: the
    26	//!   wire format owns its strings; the runtime types share via Arc once
    27	//!   inside the workbook.
    28	//! - `ValueWire` and `NamedTargetWire` are reused from
    29	//!   `ql_io::qbook_format` (Phase 2A.8 shipped them). Sharing the
    30	//!   serialization vocabulary across the engine's three persistence
    31	//!   surfaces (qbook envelope, qbook JSONL, op log) prevents wire-format
    32	//!   drift.
    33	
    34	use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
    35	
    36	use crate::wire::{CellWireValue, NamedTargetWire};
    37	use ql_types::{ColId, RowId, SheetId};
    38	
    39	/// A mutation operation recordable in the op log.
    40	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    41	#[serde(deny_unknown_fields, tag = "kind")]
    42	#[non_exhaustive]
    43	pub enum Op {
    44	    /// Single literal cell write. Mirrors `WorkbookRuntime::set_value`.
    45	    PutValue {
    46	        sheet: SheetId,
    47	        row: RowId,
    48	        col: ColId,
    49	        value: CellWireValue,
    50	    },
    51	
    52	    /// Single formula write (text only — replay re-evaluates via the
    53	    /// caller's `WorkbookRuntime::recompute_all`, not via replay itself).
    54	    /// Mirrors `WorkbookRuntime::set_formula` for the persistence side.
    55	    PutFormula {
    56	        sheet: SheetId,
    57	        row: RowId,
    58	        col: ColId,
    59	        text: String,
    60	    },
    61	
    62	    /// Remove formula association at a cell (cell becomes literal-only).
    63	    /// Mirrors `Workbook::clear_formula`.
    64	    ClearFormula {
    65	        sheet: SheetId,
    66	        row: RowId,
    67	        col: ColId,
    68	    },
    69	
    70	    /// Register a defined name. Mirrors `Workbook::set_name` when
    71	    /// `scope` is `None`, and `Sheet::set_scoped_name` when `scope` is
    72	    /// `Some(sheet_id)`. **W5-92 (Phase 4.6.D)** added the optional
    73	    /// `scope` field per design doc § 8.2 / Codex MEDIUM-4: a single
    74	    /// op variant covers both scopes rather than two separate ops.
    75	    ///
    76	    /// Wire compat: `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]`
    77	    /// keeps v3+old ops deserializing unchanged (missing field → `None`
    78	    /// = workbook scope, the historical behavior). Sheet-scoped names
    79	    /// emit the field; workbook-scoped names omit it.
    80	    ///
    81	    /// Replay: routes to `Workbook::set_name` (scope `None`) or
    82	    /// `Sheet::set_scoped_name` (scope `Some`); reserved-name refusal
    83	    /// surfaces as `ReplayError::NameRejected` in either path. A
    84	    /// `scope: Some(id)` referencing an unknown sheet surfaces as
    85	    /// `ReplayError::InvalidSheet`.
    86	    SetName {
    87	        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    88	        scope: Option<SheetId>,
    89	        name: String,
    90	        target: NamedTargetWire,
    91	    },
    92	
    93	    /// Create a new sheet. Mirrors `Workbook::add_sheet_with_chunk_rows`.
    94	    /// Sheet IDs are deterministic (the workbook assigns them in append
    95	    /// order); the producer doesn't pin the ID in the op, since replay
    96	    /// against an empty workbook produces the same sequence.
    97	    AddSheet { name: String, chunk_rows: u32 },
    98	
    99	    /// **W5-91 (Phase 4.6.C):** rename an existing sheet by id. Mirrors
   100	    /// `Workbook::rename_sheet`. Per design doc § 3.1, the producer-side
   101	    /// runtime rewrites all stored formula text BEFORE emitting this op
   102	    /// (and emits `Op::PutFormula` ops for each rewritten cell in the
   103	    /// same `BatchCommit`), so replay-on-top-of-snapshot OR replay-from-
   104	    /// empty both produce correct state.
   105	    ///
   106	    /// **Phase 5.3 step 2 audit closure (Codex MEDIUM-2, 2026-05-20):**
   107	    /// `old_name` is now ADVISORY at replay time, not validated. Pre-
   108	    /// step-2 the replay handler rejected ops whose `old_name` didn't
   109	    /// match the current sheet name (`SheetRenameNameMismatch`); under
   110	    /// CRDT merge of concurrent renames the second op's `old_name`
   111	    /// legitimately fails to match, so the validation was wrong. Post-
   112	    /// step-2 the replay handler unconditionally renames the sheet at
   113	    /// `id` to `new_name` (with D-2-style auto-disambiguation on
   114	    /// duplicate targets). `old_name` is retained on the wire for:
   115	    ///   - diagnostics in `ReplayError::SheetRenameRejected.old_name`
   116	    ///   - producer-side single-writer log debuggability (the op records
   117	    ///     what the producer's local state was at write time)
   118	    ///   - future re-introduction of strict-mode replay if needed
   119	    ///
   120	    /// **Trade-off**: single-writer malformed logs (`old="Wrong",
   121	    /// new="X"` against current "S") now silently rename "S" → "X"
   122	    /// rather than erroring. The CRDT use case requires this; we
   123	    /// accept the single-writer trade-off because the producer-side
   124	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::rename_sheet` validates locally before
   125	    /// emitting, so a malformed op indicates corruption or a custom
   126	    /// op-log producer that bypassed the runtime.
   127	    RenameSheet {
   128	        id: SheetId,
   129	        old_name: String,
   130	        new_name: String,
   131	    },
   132	
   133	    /// Register a format string at a specific id. Mirrors
   134	    /// `FormatTable::register_at`. Emitted when
   135	    /// `WorkbookRuntime::intern_format` allocates a NEW id; idempotent
   136	    /// on replay (re-registering an existing id with the same string
   137	    /// is a no-op). Re-registering at the same id with a DIFFERENT
   138	    /// string fails at `FormatTable::register_at` and surfaces as
   139	    /// `ReplayError::FormatRejected`. Shipped W5-80 (Phase 4.5.D part 4).
   140	    ///
   141	    /// **Phase 5.2 D-1 step 4 (2026-05-20):** `id` changed from `u32`
   142	    /// to [`crate::wire::FormatIdWire`] (tagged tuple `Builtin(u32)` /
   143	    /// `Custom(PeerId, u32)`). The `from_u32_legacy` migration helper
   144	    /// converts pre-5.2 saves to the new shape at envelope-load time
   145	    /// (step 5).
   146	    RegisterFormat {
   147	        id: crate::wire::FormatIdWire,
   148	        string: String,
   149	    },
   150	
   151	    /// Set or clear a cell's format id. Mirrors
   152	    /// `CellFormatOverlay::set` (when `id` is `Some`) and `::clear`
   153	    /// (when `id` is `None`). The id MUST resolve in the workbook's
   154	    /// `FormatTable` at replay time; an unknown id surfaces as
   155	    /// `ReplayError::FormatNotRegistered`. Shipped W5-80.
   156	    ///
   157	    /// **Phase 5.2 D-1 step 4 (2026-05-20):** `id` changed from
   158	    /// `Option<u32>` to `Option<FormatIdWire>`.
   159	    SetCellFormat {
   160	        sheet: SheetId,
   161	        row: RowId,
   162	        col: ColId,
   163	        /// `None` ⇒ clear the overlay entry (cell falls back to General).
   164	        /// `Some(id)` ⇒ bind the cell to that format id.
   165	        id: Option<crate::wire::FormatIdWire>,
   166	    },
   167	
   168	    /// One transaction's ops applied atomically at replay time. Produced
   169	    /// by `WorkbookTransaction::commit` in 2A.3.b. Replay applies each
   170	    /// inner op in order; on failure, replay reports the inner op's
   171	    /// index relative to the BatchCommit's parent index. No implicit
   172	    /// rollback at replay (commits are committed).
   173	    ///
   174	    /// Nested BatchCommits are permitted by the schema but produced
   175	    /// nowhere in production; replay handles them via recursion.
   176	    BatchCommit { ops: Vec<Op> },
   177	
   178	    /// **W5-118 (Phase 4.8.H):** create a workbook-scoped table.
   179	    /// Mirrors `WorkbookRuntime::create_table`. Replay inserts the
   180	    /// table into `Workbook::tables_mut()` after validating the same
   181	    /// invariants as the producer-side (non-overlapping footprint,
   182	    /// non-empty unique column names, table-name uniqueness against
   183	    /// both `TableTable` and `NameTable`).
   184	    ///
   185	    /// Column ids are NOT serialized — replay allocates fresh ids from
   186	    /// the workbook's `next_column_id` counter, matching producer-side
   187	    /// allocation order (deterministic if the op log is replayed in
   188	    /// order).
   189	    CreateTable {
   190	        name: String,
   191	        sheet: SheetId,
   192	        top_row: RowId,
   193	        top_col: ColId,
   194	        rows: u32,
   195	        cols: u32,
   196	        has_header: bool,
   197	        has_totals: bool,
   198	        column_names: Vec<String>,
   199	    },
   200	
   201	    /// **W5-118 (Phase 4.8.H):** drop a table's metadata. Cells inside
   202	    /// the table footprint are untouched. Formulas referencing the
   203	    /// dropped table re-bind to `BindError::UnknownTable` on next
   204	    /// recompute. Mirrors `WorkbookRuntime::drop_table`.
   205	    DropTable { name: String },
   206	
   207	    /// **W5-119 (Phase 4.8.I):** rename a table.
   208	    ///
   209	    /// Producer side ALSO rewrites stored formula text (Excel canon —
   210	    /// per design § 12.2). The op carries `old_name` so replay can
   211	    /// re-key the `TableTable` entry; producer-side formula-text

   214	    /// replay-order determinism).
   215	    ///
   216	    /// Replay validates: target name available (NameTable + TableTable),
   217	    /// source name exists. Sheet-scoped names use the workbook's
   218	    /// shared canonical namespace.
   219	    RenameTable { old_name: String, new_name: String },

    80	/// Phase 2A.8 (megaudit M11): added `Pending` variant for formula-bearing cells
    81	/// whose saved value would be `Value::Blank` (because the formula hasn't been
    82	/// evaluated yet, or its result really is Blank). Previously this case was
    83	/// encoded as `Error("#NULL!")`, conflating "not yet evaluated" with a real
    84	/// Excel `#NULL!` error. The v2 reader recognizes `Pending` as "needs
    85	/// recompute"; for v1 files, the loader auto-rewrites `Error(#NULL!)` to
    86	/// `Pending` when the same cell has a `formula` field.
    87	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq)]
    88	pub enum CellWireValue {
    89	    Number(f64),
    90	    Boolean(bool),
    91	    Text(String),
    92	    /// Stored as the canonical "#REF!" / "#VALUE!" / etc. text form.
    93	    Error(String),
    94	    /// Formula-bearing cell with no evaluated value. Recompute resolves it.
    95	    Pending,
    96	}
    97	
    98	impl CellWireValue {
    99	    /// Project a runtime `Value` into the wire shape, or `None` for `Blank`
   100	    /// (which is encoded as the absence of a `CellRecord` entry on disk).
   101	    pub fn from_value(v: &Value) -> Option<Self> {
   102	        match v {
   103	            Value::Blank => None,
   104	            Value::Number(n) => Some(CellWireValue::Number(*n)),
   105	            Value::Boolean(b) => Some(CellWireValue::Boolean(*b)),
   106	            Value::Text(s) => Some(CellWireValue::Text(s.as_ref().to_owned())),
   107	            Value::Error(e) => Some(CellWireValue::Error(error_to_canonical_text(*e))),
   108	        }
   109	    }
   110	
   111	    /// Decode the wire value into a `ql_types::Value`. `Pending` decodes to
   112	    /// `Value::Blank` — the cell is in a transient state until recompute runs.
   113	    pub fn to_value(&self) -> Result<Value, WireDecodeError> {
   114	        match self {
   115	            CellWireValue::Number(n) => Ok(Value::number(*n)),
   116	            CellWireValue::Boolean(b) => Ok(Value::Boolean(*b)),
   117	            CellWireValue::Text(s) => Ok(Value::text(s.as_str())),
   118	            CellWireValue::Error(s) => match parse_canonical_error_text(s) {
   119	                Some(e) => Ok(Value::Error(e)),
   120	                None => Err(WireDecodeError::UnknownErrorSigil {
   121	                    sigil: s.to_owned(),
   122	                }),
   123	            },
   124	            CellWireValue::Pending => Ok(Value::Blank),
   125	        }

   390	    Ok(count)
   391	}

   393	/// Recursive helper. `index` is the op's position in the outer log (or
   394	/// the synthetic position of the enclosing BatchCommit for nested ops —
   395	/// 2A.3.a flattens by reporting the parent's index for nested failures).
   396	fn apply_op(op: &Op, workbook: &mut Workbook, index: usize) -> Result<(), ReplayError> {

     1	//! `Value` — the Phase 0 minimum cell-value type per spec Part V §2.
     2	//!
     3	//! Five variants — `Blank`, `Number(f64)`, `Boolean(bool)`, `Text(Arc<str>)`, `Error(ErrorValue)`.
     4	//! No `Int`, `Date`, `Time`, `Array`, or `Pending` for Phase 0 — Excel itself stores numbers
     5	//! as f64, and dates as f64 serials. Date/time helpers will live in `ql-functions` once
     6	//! they're needed; Phase 0 acceptance is arithmetic-only.
     7	//!
     8	//! `Text` is `Arc<str>` so that string cells reachable from many formulas (e.g. a column of
     9	//! `=$A$1 & "x"`) clone in 16 bytes instead of duplicating the underlying buffer.
    10	//!
    11	//! Invariants enforced by constructors (NOT `Value::Number(x)` directly):
    12	//! - `Value::number()` rejects NaN and ±∞ — those map to `Error(ErrorValue::Num)` at the
    13	//!   coercion boundary (see `coercion::sanitize_f64`). Constructing `Value::Number(NaN)`
    14	//!   directly is legal at the *type* level (so callers can still build it during parsing if
    15	//!   needed); the rule is: values produced by kernels should pass through `sanitize_f64`
    16	//!   before being wrapped.

    22	
    23	/// Cell value. Five variants; nothing larger than a fat pointer at runtime
    24	/// (Number = 8 bytes, Text = 16-byte Arc, Boolean = 1 byte, Blank/Error = 1 byte each;
    25	/// `size_of::<Value>()` is 24 bytes on 64-bit including the discriminant + padding).
    26	///
    27	/// `Default` is `Blank` (matches Excel's empty-cell semantics).
    28	///
    29	/// **Stability:** NOT `#[non_exhaustive]`. Variants map to Excel's
    30	/// canonical value types and are semantically stable. Adding a new
    31	/// variant (e.g. first-class references, inline arrays, lambda
    32	/// values) is a MAJOR-version bump (0.x → 0.y) — `_ => ...` catch-all
    33	/// arms would conflict with the no-fallbacks rule by hiding new
    34	/// variants from existing pattern matches.
    35	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
    36	pub enum Value {
    37	    /// Empty cell (`Blank`). Coerces to 0.0 numerically, "" textually, false logically.
    38	    #[default]
    39	    Blank,
    40	    /// IEEE 754 double — Excel's canonical numeric type. NaN/Inf are valid f64 bit patterns
    41	    /// but represent broken inputs; `coercion::sanitize_f64` converts them to
    42	    /// `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num)`.
    43	    Number(f64),
    44	    /// `TRUE` / `FALSE`. Numerically coerces to 1.0 / 0.0.
    45	    Boolean(bool),
    46	    /// String value. `Arc<str>` for cheap clones across formula evaluations.
    47	    Text(Arc<str>),
    48	    /// One of the 14 error sigils — `#REF!`, `#VALUE!`, … `#AI_NOT_AVAILABLE_V1`. See `ErrorValue`.
    49	    Error(ErrorValue),
    50	}
    51	
    52	impl Value {
    53	    /// Construct a `Number`, sanitizing NaN/Inf into `Error(Num)`. Use this in kernels.
    54	    pub fn number(n: f64) -> Self {
    55	        if n.is_nan() || n.is_infinite() {
    56	            Value::Error(ErrorValue::Num)
    57	        } else {
    58	            Value::Number(n)
    59	        }
    60	    }
    61	
    62	    /// Construct a `Text` from anything `Into<Arc<str>>`. `&str`, `String`, and `Arc<str>`
    63	    /// all work; the underlying buffer is shared where possible.
    64	    pub fn text(s: impl Into<Arc<str>>) -> Self {
    65	        Value::Text(s.into())
    66	    }
    67	
    68	    /// True iff `self` is a `Value::Error`.
    69	    pub fn is_error(&self) -> bool {
    70	        matches!(self, Value::Error(_))
    71	    }
    72	
    73	    /// True iff `self` is `Value::Blank`.
    74	    pub fn is_blank(&self) -> bool {
    75	        matches!(self, Value::Blank)
    76	    }
    77

    79	    pub fn as_error(&self) -> Option<ErrorValue> {
    80	        match self {
    81	            Value::Error(e) => Some(*e),
    82	            _ => None,
    83	        }
    84	    }
    85	}

    87	impl fmt::Display for Value {
    88	    /// Invariant text representation. NOT locale-aware — for that, see future
    89	    /// `ql-functions::format`.
    90	    /// - `Blank` → ""
    91	    /// - `Number(n)` → Rust's `{n}` (no thousands separators)
    92	    /// - `Boolean(b)` → "TRUE"/"FALSE" (Excel canonical case)
    93	    /// - `Text(s)` → the string body verbatim
    94	    /// - `Error(e)` → the sigil (e.g. "#REF!")
    95	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
    96	        match self {
    97	            Value::Blank => Ok(()),
    98	            Value::Number(n) => write!(f, "{n}"),
    99	            Value::Boolean(true) => f.write_str("TRUE"),
   100	            Value::Boolean(false) => f.write_str("FALSE"),
   101	            Value::Text(s) => f.write_str(s),
   102	            Value::Error(e) => write!(f, "{e}"),
   103	        }
   104	    }
   105	}
   106	
   107	/// Structural equality. NaN follows IEEE rules — `Number(NaN) != Number(NaN)`. This matches
   108	/// Excel: NaN is never produced via the public constructor (`Value::number` sanitizes it),
   109	/// so the only way to reach `Number(NaN) == Number(NaN)` is by bypassing it, in which case
   110	/// returning `false` matches f64 semantics.
   111	impl PartialEq for Value {
   112	    fn eq(&self, other: &Self) -> bool {
   113	        match (self, other) {
   114	            (Value::Blank, Value::Blank) => true,
   115	            (Value::Number(a), Value::Number(b)) => a == b,
   116	            (Value::Boolean(a), Value::Boolean(b)) => a == b,
   117	            (Value::Text(a), Value::Text(b)) => a == b,
   118	            (Value::Error(a), Value::Error(b)) => a == b,
   119	            _ => false,
   120	        }

- Test glob `out/test/quantbook*.test.js` includes flat future names like `quantbook-transport-*.test.js`; it only misses nested subdirectories.
- Verification run: IDE quantbook Mocha suite passed `10/10`; engine `cargo test -p ql-bindings-node` passed `2/2` plus doctests.
